863. MARY ANN EVETTS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of January, 2 blankets, value 5s., the goods of Francis Gray.
FRANCIS GRAY. I am a mason. The prisoner lodged with me; the blankets were lost on the 17th of January.
CHARLOTTE GRAY. I am the prosecutor's wife. I know the blankets - I never authorised the prisoner to pawn them, but I knew of it afterwards.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not go with me to pawn them? A. I did go with a bundle, but I did not know my blankets were in it.
FRANCIS PARKER. The prisoner pawned a blanket and pillow on the 17th of January, and another blanket on the 13th of February; the prosecutor's wife used to come with her sometimes, and I believe she was with her on the 17th of January; I had not time to say this before the Magistrate.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-185]
864. THOMAS HEDGES was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of February, I bee-hive, value 2s., the goods of John Fauch.
JOHN FAUCH. I live at Harrow on the Hill. I lost a bee-hive on the 28th of February from my yard - it was found on the prisoner's premises on the 13th of March; he lives about three miles from me; this is my hive - it was a hive of bees when I lost it - it was not so bad as it is now.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Is not one hive exceedingly like another? A. Yes; but I know this by its form, a chalk line round it, and this mark on the top; this line I cut off one I work with; the prisoner said he bought it in the December preceding of his son-in-law, John West.
RICHARD WALTER. I am an officer. I found this hive between the cieling and the roof of the prisoner's kitchen, on the 9th of March - there were two hives there; he said he bought one about a month before, and the other before Christmas, of his son-in-law, and the mice had destroyed the bees.
JOSEPH DREW. I apprehended the prisoner - he said he bought a hive o bees of his son-in-law, West, about a month before, and the other before Christmas; West has absconded - he boarded with the prisoner, but I do not think he lived with him.
Prisoner's Defence. I gave 12s. for them.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-186]
865. DAVID ALLEN LORRIMER was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, I coat, value 12s., the goods of Philip Phipps.
PHILIP PHIPPS. I am a helper in a stable. I lost my coat on the 6th of March from my bed-room, over the coach-house, in Grantham-mews; the prisoner slept withme - I saw my coat safe the same day, and missed it between three and four o'clock in the afternoon - the prisoner was then gone; I lent it to him once or twice - if he had asked me for it I should have lent it him.
JOHN BROWN. I slept in the same room - I saw the prisoner that morning, he said he was going to see a gentleman about a fresh service; he borrowed my shoes - I saw him take the coat and go out.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-187]
866. JOHN NEWMAN and JAMES COLE were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 1 box, value 6s.; 6 spoons, value 20s.; 3 rings, value 15s.; 2 brooches. value 20s.; 2 pairs of ear-rings, value 4d.; 1 shell, value 3d.; 1 buckle, value 4d., and 2 bands, value 3d., the goods of Elizabeth Chasey.
ELIZABETH CHASEY. I am single. On the 26th of February, I left a box, containing this property, in a hackney coach, which took me to No. 4, Montague-place; I did not miss it till the evening following - these are the articles; Newman was the end, and Cole was the coachman.
MARTHA WIMPSEY. On the 26th of February Chasey left my lodgings in Little Coram-street. in a coach which Cole drove - Newman was the cad, but I did not see him on the box; I saw the box which contained these spoons, rings, brooches, and other things.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see these things put into the coach? A. Yes, by Newman; Cole assisted him in bringing down one heavy box, that was all I saw Cole do; the box containing these articles was a small one of six or seven inches square.
GEORGE COST. I am a Bow-street officer. Early on the morning of the 2d of March, I went to the Lamb public-house, in Lamb's Conduit-street - I inquired for the cad -I saw Newman. and took him into custody; I had taken Cole the same morning; I got this reticule from Mary Wells, who said that Newman gave it her - and these other articles I got from a Mrs. Taylor, who said Newman gave them to her for the children to play with; I got these spoons, two rings, and the brooch from Bolt.
Prisoner NEWMAN. The witness and the prosecutrix came to me, and asked if I knew where the box was; I said No; and they said, so help their god, if I would tell them where it was, they would forgive me. Witness. There was a notice stuck up, that if any person would bring these things they should be forgiven; but Newman denied all knowledge of it; he was described as a person with a red waistcoat and a drab coat; Cole bears a good character, and said he should have given up the things had it not been for Newman.
JOHN BOLT. I am a hackney-coachman. On the last Saturday night in February Cole left a small parcel with me, and asked me to take care of it - there were some silver spoons and other things; I cannot swear that these are the spoons - I did not look at the marks.
Cross-examined. Q. Have not hackney-coachmen four days to leave at the office any thing they find? A. Yes, four clear days - that is what I have been given to understand; I have deposited things there myself.
NEWMAN's Defence. The lady came to me about seven o'clock, and asked the man, who stood first, to be at Little Coram-street at seven; I went, and told Cole to go; he said, "I will," and I said I would go and put the luggage in - there was a large box which he could not get in himself, and I rode with him to get it out; when we took the luggage out, we asked the prosecutrix if it was all right - she said Yes; I asked her to give me 6d., and the coachman stood murmuring for 6d. more, but the door was shut in our faces - I did not know this was in the coach.
NEWMAN - GUILTY, Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
COLE - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 3 Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-188]
Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
867. WILLIAM SANDERS and WILLIAM BURROWS were indicted for stealing, on the 27th of March, 2 deals, value 16s., the goods of Robert Eckett.
JAMES MARTIN. I am in the service of Robert Eckett, a builder. These deals were in his timber-yard, in Battlebridge-fields, at half-past five o'clock, on the 27th of March; when I left the yard, I saw the two prisoners watching me - Sanders had worked in the yard as a sawyer the week before; I waited about fifty yards off, and saw Sanders go and try the gate which I had fastened - he got over the pales, and unfastened it; Burrows who was standing outside, then went in - I went into a house, where I could look into the yard, and saw them looking over a small pile of deals; they looked out two, and then proceeded to line them as if for cutting - they then put them on the pit; I went into another house, where I could see the pit, and soon after they took away the deals, and carried them across a field - I sent for an officer; we went round behind a large brick-kiln in the field, and saw the prisoners with the deals - these are them; they had no right to take them - they had been at the yard about an hour before, and asked if there was any stuff to be cut; I said there was a plank, not a deal - they asked what time I left work, and quitted the premises; I saw them no more till half-past five o'clock: Burrows is a shoemaker.
BURROWs' Defence. I was employed to carry them; I did not know they were stolen.
SANDERs' Defence. He ordered the deals to be cut, and we were going to another pit to cut them; I was tipsy.
SANDERS - GUILTY. Aged 23.
BURROWS - GUILTY. Aged 23.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-189]
868. JOHN SEALES was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 3 bushels of potatoes, value 5s., the goods of Jeffery Smith, his master.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
ROBERT PARKER. I live in Shoreditch, and deal in potatoes. On the 6th of March I purchased twenty-four tons of potatoes, at a wharf in Tooley-street; they were to be taken to my premises in Shoreditch - I left the wharf about five o'clock in the afternoon; a waggon, which the prisoner drove, and a cart driven by another man, were then loaded - I did not count how many sacks were placed in the waggon, but it should carry forty-eight sacks; a note was given with it: the prisoner came with his waggon to my premises about seven o'clock in the evening, and I saw the potatoes delivered - Pearce and two or three others of my servants helped him to unload, and carry them in; before they were unloaded, I asked the prisoner whereHolmes, the man who drove the cart, was - he said he had not seen him; I said it was very strange, as the cart started first, and ought to have arrived first: when the sacks were emptied from the waggon, they were placed in the shop, not in the street - I saw there were but forty-seven; I got into the waggon, and saw an empty sack lying in it - the prisoner was in the waggon; I accused him of having the empty sack there, and offering my man gin to carry it in - he denied it, and said he knew nothing about it: the value of the potatoes is about 5s.; the prisoner is carman to John Smith, a wharfinger.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You do not know whether he saw the waggon loaded? A. I do not know; there was a ticket given to the prisoner - that ticket is not here.
JOHN COSBUR. I am a servant to Mr. Jeffery Smith. I remember forty-eight sacks of potatoes being put into the prisoner's waggon - I helped to load them; the prisoner was not near at the time, but I saw him drive the waggon away, after it had stood there about three hours.
Cross-examined. Q. What is Mr. Smith's name? A. Jeffery; I have seen his name on the waggon - I was not at the wharf all the time; after the waggon was loaded I was backwards and forwards - there were a great many people in the wharf, perhaps a hundred, doing their work; I recollect the prisoner having two hundred and five cheeses booked to him, and he delivered two hundred and fourteen- I cannot say whether there were forty-eight sacks in the waggon when it went away.
BENJAMIN MOXON. I am clerk to Mr. Jeffery Smith- he has no other name, nor partner.
WILLIAM PEARCE. I am servant to Mr. Palmer. On the night in question the prisoner brought the potatoes to my master's; I assisted to unload them - when I had got eight or ten sacks out of the waggon, the prisoner said "I have an empty sack and I will put it into the mouth of your sack, to make all right," I said, I should not do any such thing - I carried in that sack of potatoes, came back, and the empty sack was in the mouth of the next whole sack I was to carry in; he said "You must do it now - you can do it if you like; I will give you a drop of gin to make it all right," I said I should do no such thing, but he must take the sack out, put it in the bottom of the waggon, and I should see if the full number were delivered, when the sacks were counted; as the sacks were emptied they were placed against the counter in the shop; I carried one or two more sacks in - the prisoner again said, "You must do it," and there was the empty sack in the month of another; I said, I should do no such thing - he took the sack out with his left hand, and threw it down into the bottom of the waggon; when the sacks were all taken out, I did not go back to the waggon - there were only forty-seven sacks carried in and emptied.
Cross-examined. Q. Whether any person might have emptied a sack of potatoes on the wharf you cannot tell? A. No.
THOMAS WILD. I am clerk to a gentleman in Tooley-street. I saw forty-eight sacks of potatoes in the waggon.
Cross-examined. Q. Were you there when the waggon was loading? A. Yes; that was between one and two o'clock; the waggon left about five - I did not stay by it, I went away; I did not see the prisoner come to it, nor see it go away; I believe there was a tarpauling over it when it stood in the yard in the afternoon.
COURT. Q. How were the sacks placed? A. Four rows of nine in each, and twelve laid at the top of them; they were not tied on the top.
Prisoner's Defence. I went with three waggons, but never loaded any one of them; the first waggon had a tarpauling, and no other - I never said any such thing to Pearce.
GUILTY. Aged 45.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-190]
869. WILLIAM HOLMES was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 3 bushels of potatoes, value 5s., the goods of Jeffery Smith, his master.
MR. ALLEY conducted the prosecution.
ROBERT PARKER. I was to have had twenty-eight sacks of potatoes brought by the prisoner's cart from Tooley-street, on the 6th of March - I did not see them in the cart so as to notice the number; when the prisoner came to my door there were but twenty-seven sacks in the cart- I said, "You have been making away with one sack;" he said he knew nothing about it, but he supposed if he had lost one, he should have to make it good; I ordered my man to get my horse - I went over the water, and brought the salesman and his clerk; when I returned there were twenty-eight sacks in the cart, but I had taken away the empty sack before I went.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see the cart loaded? A. No; I saw it when it was loaded, but did not count the sacks - he brought a book with the potatoes; I cannot tell who loaded it - I was in the ship at the time it was loaded; I saw the prisoner starting with the cart full - twenty-four is the usual number for a cart to carry; it was overloaded - I think the sacks were not fastened with any ropes; I should think one could not have fallen off, for they can load thirty-two sacks in a cart: I suppose the distance is a mile and a half over the stones.
JOHN COSBUR. I am in the employ of Mr. Jeffery Smith. There were twenty-eight sacks of potatoes put into the prisoner's cart.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see them loaded? A. No, but I counted them when he went out of the wharf; I did not get into the cart, but I could tell if I were blindfolded pretty near - some of them did partly conceal others, but not entirely.
GEORGE STEVENS. I am a servant to Mr. Adams, a butcher. I saw the prisoner with a cart, near my master's shop, in Shoreditch, about half-past seven o'clock in the evening; I saw him at the off-side of the cart, with a sack of potatoes on his shoulder - he tried to put it on, but he could not, and he put them down, and beckoned to two respectable men to give him a lift, but they would not - he then beckoned to two bricklayer's labourers, and they lifted it on the wheel; he got into the cart, put it in, and then went on towards Mr. Parker's.
Cross-examined. Q. How far was this from Mr. Parker's? A. Not more than half a dozen doors - it was before he got to Mr. Parker's; the cart did not go on, but he went in - whether it fell off or not I cannot tell; the cart seemed to he pretty full.
COURT. Q. Was the sack tied? A. I did not notice that.
Prisoner's Defence. There were twenty-eight sacks when I got to Mr. Parker's.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-191]
870. WILLIAM DUGDALE was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Robert Tate, from his person.
ROBERT TATE. I am a goldsmith, and live in Regent-street. On the 8th of April, between eleven and twelve o'clock, I was in Conduit-street; I was followed by two suspicious looking lads - I felt for my handkerchief, and it was safe; they got behind me - I felt again, and it was gone; I turned and seized the prisoner, who was nearest to me, and took it from his breast - it was mine; I have not brought it here.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going up the street, and two boys let this handkerchief fall; I took it up, and put it in my breast.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18290409-192]
871. ALICE ALLISON was indicted for stealing. on the 10th of March, 1 blanket, value 5s., and 2 sheets, value 10s., the goods of William Winder.
WILLIAM WINDER. I keep a public-house, in Gray's Inn-lane; the prisoner took a room there, on the 7th of March; on the 10th I was out for my pots, and saw her in the street; I followed her down Fox's-court, and saw a blanket hanging down - the street-keeper came by, and I gave charge of her.
ANN WINDER. This is my husband's blanket; I know it.
Prisoner's Defence. I had just come out of the poor-house, and I did it to get a bit of muslin, to make some caps, which I had an order for, to enable me to pay my rent; I should have put it on the bed again in a night or two.
GUILTY. Aged 60.
Confined Eighteen Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-193]
872. JOSEPH HOWELL was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of March, 7 lbs. weight of hog's lard, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Moger.
THOMAS MOGER. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Whitecross-street. I had seen the prisoner pass my shop several times, on the evening of the 4th of March: about nine o'clock, he took a bladder of lard, which was inside my shop, but the window was open - the watchman stopped him with it.
SAMUEL YATES. On the night of the 4th, I saw this boy and another going along Whitecross-street; I watched them for a time, and then lost sight of them - I saw the prisoner with something in his hand; I asked what he had got - he said he did not know; I found this lard.
MARY JACKMAN. I saw the prisoner take this lard from the window; another boy, in a brown coat, stood in the road to look while he took it.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Whipped and Discharged.

873. JOHN MCNAMARA was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, I shovel, value 2s., the goods of John Ivers; I shovel, value 2s., the goods of William Ivers; and 1 jacket, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Southcott .
JOHN IVERS. I am a labourer. I lost a shovel on the 5th of March, from Battle-bridge, where I was at work for Mr. Southcott - this is it.
GEORGE WADDINGTON. I am an officer. On the 5th of March I was in the New-road, and saw the prisoner with these two shovels and this jacket; I took him to the office - he asked to go backwards, and put this jacket down the privy.
THOMAS SOUTHCOTT. This jacket is mine; I lost it from Mr. Jackson's yard, on the 5th of March - I never saw the prisoner.
The prisoner pleaded poverty.
GUILTY. Aged 27.

[trialAccount t18290409-195]
874. MARY ANN PEARCE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 pair of gloves, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Henry Simpson.
SARAH ANN SIMPSON. I am the wife of Henry Simpson, a glover, of New-street, Covent-garden. The prisoner, (Lady Barrymore,) came to our shop last Tuesday morning, about half-past ten o'clock, and asked for a pair of gloves; I knew her, and was frightened at her, and told her we had not any that would suit her; she said she must have a pair - she was rather tipsy; she stood some minutes, and did not say any thing - she then said again she must have a pair; I told her she had better wait till Mr. Simpson came home, and perhaps he would give her a pair; she said she would not, but she would take these - she took a pair off the counter, and walked away; I had not dealt with her before, but she came to the shop during the very cold weather - I did not know where she lived; she had had a pair the night before, and paid for them.
WILLIAM RILEY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner in James-street, Covent-garden, and found these gloves on her; she appeared very drunk, but she makes herself so sometimes, and you cannot tell whether she is or is not.
Prisoner's Defence. I went in there the evening before, and purchased a pair of gloves; to the best of my recollection I paid 1s. 4d. for them, and he told me any time I wanted a pair, I was welcome to them - I do not know what made me do it; I am not in the habit of going in there - I have no recollection of being in the office till I was before the Magistrate.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-196]
875. JAMES REEVES and CHARLES SMITH were indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 4 candlesticks, value 4s., the goods of George Kerr.
EDWARD QUIN. I am a wine-cooper. Last Monday I saw Smith take a parcel from under the window of Mr. Kerr, a broker, at Poplar; I put down my truck, and took him with a pair of candlesticks; I asked what he had got - he said, "A pair of candlesticks," and I took him back; in about ten minutes I met the officer with the other prisoner.
THOMAS GRAY. I am an officer. I saw Smith and Reeves in company at Poplar; they separated, and I went after Smith - I went and got these candlesticks of Mr. Kerr, and took Smith into custody.
ROBERT MARSTON. I am an officer. I took Reeves on the same day; I found this pair of candlesticks under his coat - he said a boy gave him them; when he was brought to Smith I asked who took them; Reeves said they took a pair a piece.
GEORGE KERR. I am a broker. These candlesticks are mine.
REEVES' Defence. I picked them up in High-street, Poplar.
The prisoners received a good character.
REEVES - GUILTY. Aged 18.
SMITH - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18290409-197]
876. THOMAS SELLON was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of April, 1 jacket, value 5s., the goods of Philip Bradley.
PHILIP BRADLEY. I am a lighterman. I lost a jacket from a ship's boat, in Blackwall basin, on the 4th of April; the prisoner brought it back when he saw me coming on shore - I have seen him about; I had only been on board the ship for a moment, and then my jacket was gone - the prisoner had it on; when he saw me coming he laid it down.
JAMES HESELTINE. I am an officer. I went in pursuit of the prisoner; he ran away, and I overtook him - he dropped this jacket.
Prisoner's Defence. I was trying to get into a ship to go to sea; I saw a person who asked for a boat - I took a boat off to him; a lad came down from a ship, and put me on shore - I said to a boy."Whose jacket is this?" he said, "It is the pilot, Bradley's, and he is on shore;" I said, "I will take it to him" - I then saw Mr. Bradley come out of the ship; I said, "Is this your jacket?" he said, "I'll jacket you;" he and his brother William began to rope's end me; I got a little from them - then they threw stones at me, and I ran to get away.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-198]
877. WILLIAM GRADY was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 1 watch, value 16l., the goods of David Hughes, from the person of John Pollock .
DAVID HUGHES. I live in Frith-street, Soho - I am a watch and clock-maker; John Pollock is my errand-boy. On the 7th of March I sent him out with three parcels, about three o'clock in the afternoon - one parcel was a watch for Mr. Walker, of Red Lion-street, Clerkenwell
JOHN POLLOCK. I shall be thirteen years of age next September - I am errand-boy to Mr. Hughes. In the afternoon of the 7th of March I received this watch to take to Mr. Walker; I got as far as Smithfield - I asked a person in the street for Greenhill's-rents, where I had to deliver a parcel: he said, "Who do you want?" - I said Mr. Willis, and somehow I mentioned my master's name, but I cannot tell why; that person then went away - a boy came up to me soon after, and said, "I have been to Mr. Hughes, and I came from Mr. Willis, I want the work;" he said he would take it to Mr. Willis, and I might go on my errands - he said, "What is for Mr. Willis?" he took the watch out of my pocket, and said,"I know this is for him" - I said, "I know it is not, if you don't give it to me I will cry thieves;" he gave me sixpence to give it to him - I threw it at him, and said, "If you know where Mr. Willis lives, tell me;" he directed me, but it was wrong - an old woman told me where it was, and I went; Mr. Willis told me to make haste home, and just as I got out of Greenhill's-rents again, I saw the boy - I said, "You thief. you only wanted to steal the things from me;" he said, "Hark at that young thief!" and then the prisoner came up and took my part - he said, "What is the matter, my little boy?" I related the circumstance to him; he said, "Come along with me, and I will see that you are not robbed" - we went on together, and then he stopped; I saw the boy come up and speak to him, and the man who had first spoken to me: they all three seemed to be talking together - the prisoner then beckoned me up to him, and said, "Is not this he?" I said Yes; he said to him, "If you don't go along I will box your ears" - the boy then went away, then the prisoner, the man and I went on a little way together; then the man went away - the prisoner and I were alone, and I asked the way to Red Lion-street, as I was going to Mr. Walker's; he went with me as far as the White Bear public-house, and he said, "Come in here, a friend of mine keeps it - the thieves will think you live here, and they will go away;" he called for a glass of gin and water, and three times he asked me to drink some - I refused, and said I was not accustomed to drink it; he asked me a fourth time, and I took it, but did not drink more than a tea-spoonful; he said, "Drink hearty" - I said I was not in the habit of it; I said, "Where is that other gentleman that you spoke to?" he said, "I expect him here presently" - he then related the circumstance to another man, who was there, and the prisoner said to me, "Let me look at the watch, won't you?" I hesitated, and he said, "Do, in your own hand" - then the other man took it, and said it was very pretty; he tried to open it, and gave it to the prisoner - the prisoner kept trying to open it, and said, "Go, and fetch your hat;" I said, "Give me the watch, if you please" - he said, "Go for your hat;" I said, "I will not go till I get the watch" - then the prisoner said he had given it me; he got up and went out, and the other man held my arm; I bit him over the knuckles, and got out - I cried Stop thief! and a gentleman ran after him; he ran up Crown-street, and a person stopped him - I came up and said, "That is the villain that has robbed me of my master's watch," and he gave it to me.
MATTHIAS SHORE. I heard Stop thief! called, and saw the prisoner running very fast; I laid hold of him by the coat - he struggled a good deal; the boy came up and said, "That's the man who stole my master's watch," and I saw the prisoner give him a bit of paper - he was given to the officer.
MARY SMITH. I keep the White Bear. I had a glimpse of a man who ran out, but I cannot say who it was; I do not know what became of the other person.
GEORGE JAMES HAZELTON. I was standing at my shop-door, at four o'clock in the afternoon, on Saturday, the 7th of March - I saw the prisoner running; I hallooed after him, and saw him taken - he said it was a boy, or some such words as that.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going by Smithfield-bars, and saw two men quarrelling with a boy; he said they wanted to rob him - I said why did he not give theminto custody; he said he was going into Smith-street, which happened to be in my way - he then said he was going to Mr. Walker's in Red Lion-street; I said I would shew him the place - he said he thought the two men were following him; I said we had better go somewhere first - we went into the White Bear; he then said that he thought he saw the two men, and he wished I would go out and take them: I ran out; they went down St. John's-lane - he went out with me; a young man stopped me.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18290409-199]
878. ELLEN TIMSON was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of March, 1 watch, value 4l.; 1 watch-ribbon, value 2d., and 1 watch-key, value 2d., the goods of John Thomas Richardson, from his person.
JOHN THOMAS RICHARDSON. I am a type-founder. On the 2d of March I and a friend were walking up Cow-lane, a little after eight o'clock in the evening - the prisoner stopped me, and asked me to give her something to drink; we all went and had a quartern of gin -I did not know her before, but as we were coming out she met another young woman, and asked if we would accompany them home; we all went together to West-street, where she said she lived - we went into a room up one pair of stairs, where there was a fire; I sat down on a chair by the fire - the prisoner's sister, (as she called her) and my friend left the room, and went into an adjoining room together; I and the prisoner were left alone - they took the light with them, but there was a good fire; the prisoner stirred up the fire, and called the servant to bring a light, which she did - the prisoner took it and put it on the table; I had my hands in my breeches pocket - the prisoner came up to me, took my watch, ran off, and pulled the door too; I ran out, and called Stop thief! my friend came. and I told him of it; she was taken in about twenty minutes, in a shop in Field-lane, but the watch has not been found - I asked her where it was - she said she had left it at that shop for 10s.; the woman said, "No, you have not" - she said, "I have;" a watchman was coming by, and I gave her into custody- she was taken to the watch-house; she there stated. that if I did not appear I should have my watch the next morning - I had not made any bargain with her, she was too much in a hurry to take my watch; I had taken no liberties with her whatever; I have a wife - she was in the country at the time.
WILLIAM MINNS. I am a letter-founder. I was with the prosecutor when we fell in with these two girls, and treated them; he was quite sober - we all went into the room together; the prisoner's friend and I went into the next room - I did not see the prisoner run out, but I heard him charge her in the street with having robbed him; we found her at the shop - she said she had left the watch there for 10s.; the woman said she had not: I heard her say at the watch-house, that if the prosecutor would not appear, the watch should be returned.
Prisoner. Q. You said, "If you return me the watch, my friend will not appear," and I said, "How can I give you the watch when I have not got it?" A. No, she said she had left it for 10s.
GEORGE CRANE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner - she had nothing about her; she said something about the watch being given up at the watch-house.
Prisoner's Defence. He was taking very indecent liberties with me; when the girl brought the candle I went out, and he said, "If it is twenty years I will have my revenge on you;" he said he felt me take the watch out of his pocket.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18290409-200]
879. THOMAS STRETTON and RICHARD FOREST were indicted for stealing, on the 4th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of Joseph Howle, from his person.
JOSEPH HOWLE. I am a tailor, and live in Warwick-street, Golden-square. I was in the Strand, about eleven o'clock in the morning of the 4th of March; a gentleman touched me, and said I had had my pocket picked - I turned, and saw my handkerchief in Stretton's hand; he was in the custody of the gentleman, who told me of it - I began to reprimand him, and the gentleman said, "That is not the one that took it - I'll fetch the man that took it;" he went and fetched the other prisoner.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did Stretton hear the conversation? A. No question of it; I did not take hold of him; he was not allowed to go any distance; he never stirred from me.
FRANCIS COSTER. I am an oilman, and live in the Strand. I was coming down Drury-lane, and saw the prosecutor - the two prisoners were behind him; Forest had his hand in his pocket, and took out the handkerchief, which he passed to Stretton; I immediately ran and seized Stretton - I told the prosecutor he had been robbed; I then ran and took Forest.
Cross-examined. Q. Had not Stretton been allowed to go a distance? A. Yes, five or six doors - that was after Mr. Howle had seen him and I had fetched Forest; he went about twenty-five yards or thereabouts - he did not attempt to run; it was not possible that the handkerchief could have dropped on the ground, and the other take it up.(Property produced and sworn to.)
FOREST'S Defence. I had been on an errand for Mr. Reece, where I work; this man came and touched me, and said he wanted me - that I was the person who had picked his pocket; Mr. Howle told me I might go about my business, but this man called me a pickpocket, and then gave me in charge.
STRETTON's Defence. I was going along, and heard a person say, "You are robbed;" I saw a handkerchief upon the ground, and picked it up - a gentleman came up, and said, "Take charge of him;" I had not my senses at the moment, I was so confused; I did drop it down - he then brought this other young man, and said I might go away, then he called me again, and said, "The other one has been saucy, and the gentleman is determined to give you both into custody."
The prisoners received a good character.
STRETTON - GUILTY. Aged 30.
FOREST - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-201]
Before Mr. Common Sergeant.
880. ANN RAYNOR was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of March. 1 shirt, value 10s., and 1 shawl, value 2s., the goods of William Arrol, her master.
WILLIAM ARROL. The prisoner came to live servant in my family about the middle of February; we missed several things, and sent for a constable - I saw him take a bundle of duplicates from a dresser-drawer which the prisoner made use of; the servant of a gentleman who lives in my house saw the bundle of duplicates, and examined some of them.
THOMAS STEVENS. I am a pawnbroker. I produce a shawl, pawned by the prisoner, on the 2d of March; I am not certain it was her.
RICHARD ROBERTS. I have a shirt, pawned by a woman on the 3d of April; I cannot be certain who it was.
GEORGE KNOX. I am a watch-house-keeper. I was sent for to the prosecutor's, and took charge of the prisoner; she gave me these duplicates, which she said she found in the drawer: two of them belonged to this property - the prisoner said the duplicates were hers, but did not say any thing about the property.
MR. ARROL. This shirt belongs to my wife's brother, who entrusted this and several other things to my care; he did not say any thing to me about the shirt, but I understood his clothes were left in my house - we had seven shirts made for him in the Burlington school, and this is one; it has never been washed.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating her intention to have redeemed the property on the receipt of her wages.
GUILTY. Aged 40.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury.
Confined Four Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-202]
881. JAMES AVERY was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of March, 1 half-pint of cauliflower seed, value 15s., and 1 quart of lettuce seed, value 20s., the goods of Daniel Carter.
MR. PHILLIPS conducted the prosecution.
DANIEL CARTER. I am a market-gardener, and live at Walham-green. The prisoner had undertaken to do some trenching work for me, for which I was to pay him 4d. a rood - it would have come to 27s., but he left it unfinished, and had drawn 22s.; he came to me for the rest of his wages, and said "You b-y thief, do you mean to pay what you owe me;" I said "Go about your business, I will not have my house disturbed;" I sent for the constable, but he was not at home - I said, "If you don't go away, I will make you;" I went to make some resistance - he took up a large hoe and swore he would knock it into me; he then swore that if he caught me or my housekeeper out at night he would murder us, and said he would rob me that night, or some night, to the amount of 10l. - I did not miss any thing till I was told by my man of it, and then missed some cauliflower seed; I went to the house at which the prisoner lodges; I went into the house with one officer and another stood outside - this bag was thrown out of the window; the officer brought it in - it is my bag and my marking; there was lettuce and cauliflower seeds in it - I had missed some seed before I went to his place; the prisoner was in the house - I saw him taken.
WILLIAM IVIMEY. I am an officer of Queen-square. I went to the house with the prosecutor; when I had been there about five minutes, a window was thrown open; I went and picked up this bag of seed - I went into the house again, and found the prisoner behind the door; he said he had taken this seed from Mr. Carter.
Prisoner's Defence. I went to work for this man, and was to do so much work at 4d. per rood - I did it; and then we were to fork it and have 4d. a rood for that - he said, "Make your bill out and bring it;" I did so - he then said "You may go and look for your money where you like, you have not finished your work, there is a bit of hedging to put up;" I said "That will not take ten minutes;" we went to do that, and he told a man that he knew very well he could keep us us out of the money for a fortnight, as we must go to Hammersmith to get a summons, which will cost a shilling - after we had finished the work I went to him again on the Sunday, and met him against the gate, and two more with him; he said I might settle it how I liked, and worse than that; I went again about eleven o'clock; he said he had not time to talk to me - I said I had neither victuals nor drink, and the place I had been at work was where I must come for money; he began to abuse me, and I did him - at last he ran for a poker to knock me away from the door; I went across the yard and took a hoe to stand in my own defence; when I came back the door was shut - I do not say but what I made use of very bad expressions, but I did not strike the house at all; I took the hoe back to its place - I saw a bag of seed, and said I will take this till he settles with me for the money; he said I must do as I liked - I left the seed upon the binding-board and went away; in the afternoon I went again for my money, and he said he would not pay me - I took the seed, held it up, and said "I shall keep this till you pay." GUILTY. Aged 28.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-203]
882. JAMES DRISCOLL was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of March, 6 lbs. weight of mutton, value 3s., the goods of William Phillips.
WILLIAM PHILLIPS. I am a butcher. On the 12th of March, about twenty minutes past nine o'clock at night, I heard a cry of Stop thief! and ran out - the officer had the prisoner with my mutton, which I had seen on the block twenty minutes before.
EDWARD COOPER. I am an officer. On the 12th of March I was on duty in Brick-lane - I saw the prisoner and two other boys; the prisoner and one more went up to the prosecutor's shop - the prisoner drew the mutton from the block and gave it another; I followed and took him; when I brought him back there was a mob round the door, and I picked up the breast of mutton.
MICHAEL CONNELL. I heard Mr. Cooper call Stop thief! I stopped the prisoner and gave him to the officer, about fifty or sixty yards from Mr. Phillips' shop; I had seen the prisoner about with some bad company.
Prisoner's Defence. Another boy took it and dropped it; I was running and was stopped.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 14.
Judgment Respited.
[trialAccount t18290409-204]
883. JAMES LEDWICH was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 1 writing-desk, value 10s., the goods of Cornelius Wallace.
REBECCA WALLACE. I am the wife of Cornelius Wallace - he is a mariner, and lives in Collins'-place, High-street, Poplar. On the 17th of March the prisoner came to me for a writing-desk of my husband's, who was on a voyage to Quebec - I let him have it, to take it to my husband.
JOHN CHUBB. I am a pawnbroker. I have a desk pawned by the prisoner on the 17th of March.
JAMES FOGG. I took the prisoner on the 18th of March, at a public-house; the publican said, "This young man was sent for a desk for the mate of the Scott, but he has never got it;" I said to the prisoner, "Did you get the desk from the mate's wife?" he said, Yes; and he had sent it on board the Scott.
Prisoner's Defence. I was with the person who pawned it, but I did not pawn it; we were going to the play, and the desk was heavy to carry - we left it there; when I was taken I thought it was gone on board.
The prisoner received an excellent character.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 1 Month.

884. JOHN ADAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of March, 1 coat, value 3l., the goods of Eli Pethwist.
WILLIAM CROOK. I am in the service of Mr. Linsall, of King-street, Holborn. Mr. Pethwist, a tailor, lived opposite to us. On the evening of the 28th of March, I saw the prisoner step into the shop, take a coat, and put it into his apron - he then gave it to another man; I went out and took the prisoner; my employer took the coat.
ELI PETHWIST. This is my coat - I received it from Mr. Linsall.
WILLIAM COTTERELL. I am beadle. I took the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was passing the shop, and came up to this witness, who charged me with stealing the coat, which I did not.
GUILTY. Aged 17.

[trialAccount t18290409-206]
885. HANNAH FLANNAGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of February, 1 half-crown, and 5s., the monies of John Redguard, from his person.
JOHN REDGUARD. On Sunday night, the 15th of February, I met the prisoner in Bunhill-row; she asked me to go home with her; I said I wanted no connexion with her - she took hold of me by the collar, and dragged me into a house in Draper's-buildings; one of them knocked me about, and the other took my money from me; they wanted me to give them some money, but I would not - I got away, and then missed half a crown and 5s.; it was all marked, and was what my master had paid me.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not say at the office that you had a cut in your head, and was very much intoxicated? A. I was intoxicated, and was sick with their knocking me about; I did tell the officer I had had a cut in my head -I offered to stop the charge, because I was afraid of losing my place.
THOMAS WALKER. I am a watch-house-keeper. I took the prisoner between one and two o'clock in the morning; I took half a crown and 5s. from her hand - she held it tight; the prosecutor wanted to make it up - he had been drinking, but not much; he offered me money to let the charge go - he said there was a half-crown and some shillings; he did not know exactly what, and that his master always marked the money.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me the money to sleep with him, and then when he was sick he got better; he asked for it back again, and I would not give it to him - he said he would swear a robbery against me.
ANN HARFORD I live in Twister's-alley; I am an unfortunate girl, and was with the prisoner at the time; the prosecutor gave me the money to go with me first, and then I gave it to her; I take my oath that he gave me the money.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-207]
886. BENJAMIN HAWKINS was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of August, 2 sovereigns, the monies of James Collard.
JAMES COLLARD. On the 10th of August I employed the prisoner to take a horse to Galley-quay, to go to Sunderland; he had two sovereigns to pay the expences - he did not put the horse on board, but put it at livery, and I had the bill to pay for it: I did not see him again till the 21st of March; I put my hand on his shoulder, and said,"Halloo, my friend!" he looked up and said, "Will you have any gin" - I said, "I will gin you to the watchman;" he said he had spent some of the money, and the rest he had lost; I found the horse at livery the day after he took it.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not send me to No. 11, Catherine-court, Trinity-square, to ascertain where the vessel laid? A. No such thing; I told you to go and put the horse on board, and I would give you half a sovereign when you had done.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me this horse, and told me to go to the place and inquire; I saw two gentlemen, who said the vessel was not to sail for three days, but I had better wait to see if the gentleman belonging to it would come there; I walked the horse about for an hour, and then the gentlemen came and said, "You had better put that horse up," which I did; I met a shopmate, and got rather too much - I do not know what became of the money, nor myself either hardly; I have since then broken my arm, and have been three months in the hospital - I have not had an opportunity of returning the money; if I had meant to have made a theft of it I should not have left the saddle and bridle in the ostler's hands.
GUILTY. Aged 38.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-208]

THOMAS PRESLAND. I saw the prisoner take the coat, and pursued him till he came to Berners-street, where he was taken - I never lost sight of him.
JOHN CHARLES MITCHELL. I was walking up the street, and met the prisoner running; I made a grasp athim, but he got by me, and threw this coat against the rails.
THOMAS WILLETT. I was in the street, about ten minutes after nine o'clock; I heard Stop thief! called, and saw the prisoner running on the other side; I went over, and took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I heard a cry of Stop thief! a man came and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 20.

889. SARAH MASON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 2 blankets, value 10s., the goods of John Walker.
ELIZA WALKER. I am the wife of John Walker, of Sutton-street, Soho. I lost a pair of blankets on the 8th of April, about half-past nine o'clock in the morning - I had seen them safe about ten minutes before, on the bed in the first floor front room; the prisoner had asked me to allow her to wait there for a friend, and I took her up stairs.
GEORGE BLACKMAN. I took charge of the prisoner at a house in Crown-street, between nine and ten o'clock in the morning; she had these blankets under her clothes - she did not give me any account of them, but she told the Magistrate she bought them of a Jew in Monmouth-street.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This woman said a man at her house wanted a girl, and if I would go he would give me a little money; I went, but did not stay long - I bought the blankets of a Jew in Monmouth-street.

890. FLORENCE McCARTY was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of March, 2 pairs of flannel drawers, value 7s.; 1 flannel shirt, value 4s., and 2 shirts, value 3s., the goods of Ellen Fortheringham.
ELLEN FORTHERINGHAM. I am a widow. On the 23d of March I went into the yard to take these things off the line, at half-past nine o'clock, and they were gone - I had seen them safe at eight; these are the articles.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house, with these things in a wet state; he said he got them from his wife in the morning in a tiff - I asked if he knew what marks were on them; he said there were no marks - I made inquiries, and found the prosecutrix.
BENJAMIN PORFITT. I was in Ratcliff-highway; the prisoner and these things were given to me in charge by Mr. Hart of Ratcliff-highway.
Prisoner's Defence. I left work about seven o'clock; a man came and stopped me, and said, "Sailor, will you buy any things?" I said."What are they?" and said I had no money; he said if I could shew him where to sell them he would give me 1s.; I took him to Mr. Hart's, whom I had bought some things of; he then said he was ashamed to take them in - I took them in, and Mr. Hart detained me.
GUILTY. Aged 30.

891. RICHARD PLATT was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of March, one 5l. Bank note, the property of William Sinclair.
WILLIAM SINCLAIR. I am a discharged soldier. On the 12th of March, I gave the prisoner a 5l. Bank note to fetch me change at the Commercial tap, as I did not know the value of change so well - I had known him about a month; he and I slept in the same room - he brought me no change, but I saw him next morning, between eight and nine o'clock; I asked him for the note or the change - he denied having had it; the number of the note begins with a 2; I am not a scholar, but I think there are four more figures.
SARAH WHITELOW. I am servant at the Commercial inn; I gave the prisoner five sovereigns for a 5l. note - he said it was for a person whom he lodged with.
JAMES GILLMAN. I took the prisoner; he said he had no note from the prosecutor, but he had it from Mrs. Brotherington; I went there, and she said she did not give it him - the prisoner then said he had it from a gentleman, but he did not know where he lived; 3s. 4 1/2d. were found upon him - this is the note; it is number 29,950.
Prisoner's Defence. I was quite intoxicated, and I do not recollect taking any note to get change.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 1 Month.

892. ALEXANDER ROSS was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of April, 2 pewter pots, value 2s., the goods of John Ware.
JOHN WARE. I am a publican. On the 10th of April the officer took the prisoner into custody in St. Katharine Docks; there were two pots of mine found on him; he said he had picked them up in the street, and a cart had gone over them.
CHARLOTTE HEIMEY. I am the wife of John Heimey; he keeps a marine-store shop in East Smithfield. The prisoner brought these pewter pots to our shop on Friday last; I did not see any name on them till the officer came in - I then saw "J. Ware" on them; I gave them to Haylock.
THOMAS HAYLOCK. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner in St. Katharine's Docks; he seemed to be beating something - I went near the place, and before I got there he was gone; I afterwards saw him go to amarine-store shop, in Nightingale-lane - these are the pots; they are squeezed double.
Prisoner's Defence. I found them in the street, and one of them was as if a wheel had gone over it.

893. MAURICE SHEEDY was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 4 iron stove-bottoms, value 3s., and 7 iron turn-buckles, value 6d., the goods of Margaret Nash.
MARGARET NASH. I am a widow, and deal in ironmongery. On the 3d of March these things were within four inches of my door; I had put them out myself - I was at my dinner, and a witness called out "Mrs. Nash, do you know the person who has taken these things from your door?" RICHARD BURTON. I saw the prisoner take these articles - he walked very fast by me, and I went and called to know if Nash knew the young man - I heard a voice say No- the prosecutrix then came out; the prisoner carried them in front of him: I followed him, and saw him drop the things.(Property produced and sworn to.)

894. SAMUEL TASKER was indicted for embezzlement.
CHARLOTTE PHILLIPS. I am the wife of Horatio Nathaniel Phillips, a publican - the prisoner was in our service as waiter; my husband is not here.
JOHN WEATHERBY. I am an officer, and took the prisoner - he asked if I had a warrant against him; I said I had not - he said he would go and see Mr. Phillips, and hear what he would say.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Then he went with you? A. Yes; Mr. Phillips asked if he had got any friends who would come forward to see the money paid that he owed him - I believe he owed him about 21l.; he said he had got nobody to come forward - Mr. Phillips ordered me to take him into custody, but he did not appear before the Magistrate; he said nothing to him about the 6s.
ANDREW WATSON. On the 25th of March, between one and two o'clock, I paid the prisoner 6s.
Cross-examined. Q. What was it for? A. For lodging; I took no receipt - I made a memorandum of it myself.
CHARLOTTE PHILLIPS re-examined. The prisoner had been in our service two years; he ought to account on Saturday night to me or my husband for monies received- he absconded on the Saturday night.
Cross-examined. Q. If the 6s. had been accounted for to Mr. Phillips it would have been just the same as if it had been paid to you? A. Yes.
NOT GUILTY.

Prisoner's Defence. I went to the bar and Mrs. Phillips gave me fifteen shillings, eight sixpences, and 1s. in copper, which I never did account for certainly; but she never gave me 1l. worth of silver.

897. ANN ANDREWS was indicted for stealing, on the 25th of March, 1 shawl, value 7s.; 3 aprons, value 2s., and 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of Betty Barnes; 1 shawl, value 15s., and 1 table-cloth, value 5s., the goods of Robert Back .
ROBERT BACK. I lost a shawl and table-cloth previous to the 25th of March, from a house where I resided; the prisoner lived there as servant to Mr. Jenkins - these are them; the table-cloth has my initials, and the shawl is like my wife's, but I will not swear positively to that.
BETTY BARNES. I lost a shawl, five aprons, and a handkerchief, from the same house; the prisoner was servant there - these are the articles.
GEORGE SHEPPERD. I am a pawnbroker. This red shawl was pawned by a woman - I cannot say by whom.
GEORGE UNDERWOOD. I am a pawnbroker. I have three aprons and a silk handkerchief; I cannot say by whom they were pawned.
RICHARD ROPE. This shawl was pawned with me by a woman; I do not know who.
SAMUEL SHARWOOD. I am a pawnbroker. This tablecloth was pawned with me - I believe by the prisoner.
ROBERT DUKE. I took up the prisoner at the house, and desired her to get me a light to look for some other property; while she was looking for the candle she went to the bottom of the kitchen stairs - I saw her throw her hand back; I went to the place and found between twenty and thirty duplicates - I said "You have just thrown these away, what did you do that for?" she said, because she did not want me to know she had them; they were for this property.
GUILTY. Aged 24.

[trialAccount t18290409-219]
898. ANN ALLGOOD and MARIA TOOMEY were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 pair oftrousers, value 2s. 5d.; 1 waistcoat, value 1s. 6d., and 1 pair of stockings, value 1s., the goods of Edward Randall.
EDWARD RANDALL. I am a painter. I lost this property on the 7th of April, from the Bag of Nails public-house, at Pimlico - I did not get drunk nor go to sleep; the articles were tied in a bundle; Toomey took them while the other was sitting alongside of me; they went away in different roads.
JAMES KING. I am a labourer. The two prisoners came up to the prosecutor in the Park, and went on to the first public-house; they staid there about half an hour, and then went on to the Bag of Nails - I did not go in at either of the houses.
CHARLES BANKS. I was at the Bag of Nails, between three and four o'clock on the 7th of April: the two prisoners and the prosecutor came in; they called for half a pint of rum - Toomey passed as his wife, and the other as his sister; a man there wanted to shake hands with the prosecutor, and the prisoners fell aboard of him; Toomey took the bundle and went out; Allgood put her hand into his pocket, and they both ran off in different directions - the prosecutor was sitting still; he was intoxicated.
JOHN WILLIAMS. I am a pawnbroker. The two prisoners pawned all this property at our house, on the 7th of April, between seven and eight o'clock in the evening; they were not tipsy.
CHARLES AUGUSTUS DORRINGTON. The prisoner Allgood was given into my charge at near twelve o'clock at night - there was nothing found on her; she was intoxicated.(Property produced and sworn to.)
ALLGOOD'S Defence. I met this man in St. James'-park - we were both very much intoxicated; he caught hold of us both, and took us to a public-house; he brought us all kinds of liquor; I do not know how I got home - but as to the property, I had none.
TOOMEY's Defence. I do not know how I came by the bundle; I think he must have given it me - I had no notion of robbing him; we had been all drinking the whole of the day, and Bankes was like the rest; we were all intoxicated.
ALLGOOD - GUILTY. Aged 27.
Confined Three Months.
TOOMEY - GUILTY. Aged 36.

899. JAMES BEDDOES was indicted for stealing, on the 23d of March, 4 caps, value 5s.; 3 petticoats, value 7s.: 1 pair of stays, value 3s.: 1 collar, value 1s., and 1 pocket, value 1s., the goods of John Rogers.
ELIZABETH ROGERS. I am the wife of John Rogers. On the 23d of March I had packed up the articles stated in a band-box, to take to the owner; I was driving them in a cart, about a quarter to nine o'clock - I have seen the articles since; these are them.
CHRISTIAN PODZUS. The watchman brought this property and the prisoner to the watch-house; I asked him what he had to say; he said Nothing.
MICHAEL LYON. I am a watchman. A gentleman brought the prisoner into Hand-court, and gave him to me, with this property; the owner was not there - I took him to the watch-house; I saw the cart, but did not see him take any thing; but that gentleman did.
Prisoner's Defence. I was crossing up Red Lion-street, and two lads were fighting in the road; one said,"I shan't carry this;" and another said, "I shan't;" I said, "What is in it;" and they said, "Will you have it;" I took it, and this gentleman took me.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.

900. ELEANOR COTTER was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 1 gown, value 4s., and 1 apron, value 6d., the goods of Sarah Porton.
MARY JACKSON. The prisoner came to me, on the 24th of February, about twelve o'clock, and asked for Louisa's things; I said I had not any of them - she then asked for Sally's blue gown and apron; I gave them to her, supposing Sarah Porton had sent her.
SARAH PORTON. I had never seen the prisoner that day; she used to work in the same house as me - I did not send her for the things.
BOYD SILVESTER. I am an officer. I took the prisoner on the 2d of March, she had this apron on - I asked where the gown was; she told me she went for some things, and got these in mistake; that Samuel Smith, who was outside, said "Keep them;" they took them to the Minories, and pawned the gown.
EDWARD PERKINS. I am a pawnbroker. I took this gown in pawn from the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)

WILLIAM PARKER. I am a pawnbroker. These shirts were pawned with me, on the evening of the 21st of March, to the best of my belief by the prisoner. I gave her this duplicate.

902. JOHN CLANCEY was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 1 silver watch, value 4l., and 1 seal, value 1l., the goods of Thomas Chapman, from his person.
JAMES LOGAN. I was in Mr. Thomas Chapman 's parlour, on the 14th of March, and saw his watch taken out of his pocket by the prisoner; there were no other persons there - the prisoner was well acquainted with his house, and I thought he took it to give to a lad at the bar, but he put it into his pocket, and went out; I told him to leave him alone, and he said it was all right: Mrs. Chapman came in and searched her husband's pockets, and sent for the watchman - I was taken up; I did not then tell her who tookit; they let me go, and then I told - the prisoner came in again, and sat down by me; I stated what I had seen, and he was taken.
CHARLES WESTBURY. I was in the parlour - I saw the prisoner put his hat under the landlord's head, as a pillow, and then he took his watch and put into in his own pocket; Logan said "What business have you with the watch? let the man alone;" the prisoner said it is all right.
WILLIAM SUMMERS. I took the prisoner, and found 4s. 6d. on him, and some halfpence; he said he had no knowledge of the watch; Logan said he thought he had done it out of a lark, and was going to leave it at the bar.
Prisoner's Defence. Westbury would swear my life away, if I had twenty lives; he is jealous of me about his wife - I came out to market, there was an uproar in the house, and the watch was gone: Logan never said I took it, till I came into the room again.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-224]
903. WILLIAM COTTERELL and THOMAS McCARTHY were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of February, 6 hanks of iron wire, value 7s., the goods of Alexander Hawkins.
ALEXANDER HAWKINS. I am an ironmonger, and live in Chiswell-street. I lost six hanks of iron wire on the 24th of February; I saw Cotterell take it - and while he took it, McCarthy came to the door and said, "Now then, come along, come along;" McCarthy took it on his shoulder; I went and took him with it; Cotterell came for some wire, and remained for it - but I am positive he gave this wire to McCarthy.
Cross-examined by MR. BARRY. Q. Had not Cotterell been bargaining for some wire? A. Yes; he had been asking for some; I had seen him over the way at my other premises, and my lad had gone over with him; I think it impossible that McCarthy could hear him ask for it - it was while my lad was gone for some wire that Cotterell gave McCarthy this; there were three candles in the shop, two in the window and one on the counter; I had been lying in wait for them - I ran out of my private door, and collared McCarthy; they could not see me.
JOSEPH DEERING. I am in the service of Mr. Hawkins. On the 24th of February, Cotterell came in the evening and asked me for a quarter of a pound of 16 or 17, meaning some wire - I said, "It is not here, you had better come over the way;" I went with him, and my master followed us - he went with me round to the private door, and I went into the shop; my master went and looked through a crack in the door - I went to serve Cotterell; he took the gauge, and said, "There is none here will do, you had better go and get some;" I went up into the kitchen, and presently I heard a scuffle and came down - I saw my master had got both the prisoners; the wire was on the shop floor.
Cross-examined. Q. You were in the shop when you gave the gange to Cotterell? A. Yes; a person going by might see the bargain we made - he appeared like a customer.
ALEXANDER HAWKINS re-examined. Q. What time was this? A. Between seven and eight o'clock in the evening; McCarthy did not run - he made no resistance; I said, "Now, you villain, I have got you at last;" he said the lad in the shop asked him to take the wire for him, but he did not say where to.
JOHN BROWN. I am an officer. I took the prisoners and property.
Cotterell put in a written paper, acknowledging the offence, and pleading poverty in extenuation.
McCARTHY's Defence. Cotterell asked me to carry the wire, and knowing him, I took it.
COTTERELL - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Three Months.
McCARTHY - GUILTY. Aged 31.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-225]

907. GEORGE GRAY and JOHN THOMAS were indicted for stealing, on the 26th of March, 1 gun, value 50s., the goods of David Farrow.
EDWARD GROVE. I am a shopman to Mr. David Farrow, salesman of High Holborn. On the 26th of March I was cleaning the window, and saw the two prisoners in company; Gray laid hold of a cord which secured this gun - I told the young man in the shop I thought they were going to steal it, and immediately I saw Thomas pass with the gun in his possession - I pursued, and caught Gray in the road; he slipped from me, and ran up a court- he was stopped there by two or three persons, but got away from them; I tried to stop him as he returned, but he struck at me - I stooped, and he struck his hand through a square of glass; he ran into Holborn, and was taken.
JOHN BROOKS. I was in the shop, and saw Thomas pass with the gun; I pursued - and as he got to Turnstile I caught him with the gun in his hand; he dropped it, and we had a severe struggle; but I brought him back.
ALFRED MARTIN. I saw Gray run up Red Lion-street pursued by the witness; I stopped him - he seemed very much flurried; I asked what was the matter - he said somebody gave him a punch of the head: I said if he would go back we would ascertain what that punch of the head meant - I took him back, and received this gun.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GRAY's Defence. I was going home, and that young man took hold of me. I pushed him away, went down a court, and was taken - I had nothing to do with it, and was not near the shop.
GRAY - GUILTY. Aged 24.
THOMAS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.

908. MARY MORRIS was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 1 cloak, value 7s.; 1 gown, value 1s.; 2 petticoats, value 1s.; 1 pair of shoes, value 1s., and 1 cap, value 1s., the goods of Elizabeth Nelder.
ELIZABETH NELDER. I am single, and lodge in Zion-terrace, Brunswick-square; the prisoner lodged in the back parlour. On the 28th of February I lost these articles; the prisoner had been there from the Saturday before, and went away on Saturday - I missed my cloak first, and the other things on the Tuesday following; they had been in my room - the prisoner was taken at her mother's in about a fortnight; I had known her before, but she was no particular friend of mine - I was in the front parlour when she went away.
SAMUEL MOUNTSTEPHENS. I heard of the robbery, and found the prisoner at No. 19, St. Helena-place, Spafields, about eleven days after she had taken the things; this gown, cap, and shoes, were on her person.
WILLIAM COOMBS. I am a shopman to Mr Fleming, a pawnbroker; the prisoner pawned a cloak, but it was redeemed the day afterwards.
HENRY HAYES. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Goswell-street. I have a white petticoat; the prisoner pawned at our house, and I think she pawned this; it is in the name of Miller, in which name she always pawned - I do not think we have another person who pawns in that name.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's. She is a girl of the town; she gave me these things when she was in bed.
The prisoner then put in a written defence, stating that the prosecutrix had sent her to pledge the property to procure victuals for them both.
ELIZABETH NELDER. Upon my solemn oath I never permitted her to pawn any thing of mine; we were not on intimate terms - we have taken something to eat together; I am under the protection of a gentleman.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Transported for Seven Years.

909. JOHN HENRY SWAKENBERG was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of March, 1 ham, value 12s., the goods of John Palin.
SARAH PALIN. I am the wife of John Palin, cheesemonger of Brunswick-street, Hackney-road. I saw the prisoner take a ham from our shop about a yard within the door at eight o'clock, in the evening of the 4th of March - I was at the parlour door; I gave the alarm, but he was not taken at that time - the ham has not been found; I am certain he is the man - he was taken on the Friday following.
Prisoner. Q. What sort of a coat had the man on? A. You had a cap, with one or two places torn the same as when you were taken.
JAMES ORFORD. I am a constable. I took the prisoner in Hackney-road; he denied the charge - Mr. Palin, who was with me, went for his wife, who came and identified him.
JOHN PALIN. When I went home this ham was missed. My wife described the man to me, and it was in consequence of her description he was taken; I suppose he was taken a quarter of a mile from my shop.
Prisoner's Defence. The officer came and said,"Where is that ham?" I said, "What ham?" and they sent for the woman, who said I was not the man; but her husband made her swear I was. GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined Six Weeks.

SIXTH DAY. WEDNESDAY, APRIL 15.
Third Middlesex Jury - before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
912. THOMAS GODWIN was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of March, 3 crowns, 12 half-crowns, 35 shillings, and 40 sixpences, the monies of John White, in his dwelling-house.
JOHN WHITE. I keep an eating-house in James-street, Oxford-street. I have known the prisoner about three years and a half; he lived with me on the 31st of March; this money was in a drawer behind the till, in a bag - it contained 5l. 10s., all in silver - but what coins I cannot say; I counted it just before I put it into the drawer; at half-past three o'clock, or a quarter to four, in the afternoon of the 31st of March, which was the last time I saw it; the prisoner was not in the house then, but came home in the evening, between six and seven, and about eleven I missed the money; the prisoner had been out - he had been at home from six to ten o'clock; I think he was at home when I missed my money - he was in a little parlour; I went into the parlour, my wife was there with him - I asked her, in his presence, if she had been to the till, and taken out the silver? she said, No; I said,"Then I am robbed;" my wife came out of the parlour into the shop; the prisoner said he was very sorry to hear it: he stopped about five minutes, and then went to bed; I went up that night to him in bed, and asked him if he had been and taken my money? he said No, and then said, "Why do you think I took it?" I said, "My suspicions are on you, Mr. Godwin;" he asked why I suspected him - I said, I considered he was the person, because there was nobody in the shop but him and my maid; he said he was innocent: after that my wife went up to him - I was not present; he dressed himself, and came down stairs; I then got the watchman to take him to the watch-house; he dressed himself of his own accord; he kept on saying he was innocent of it - he came back again; he was not searched at the watch-house; he stopped in the house a little while, and then I took him and the servant to the watch-house again; he kept denying it; I brought them both back, and then he went to bed - he got up about eight o'clock next morning; the officer came and saw him in the parlour; I saw the officer search him in the dining-room, and find 9s. 5 3/4d. in his pocket - then he took off his trousers and found three sovereigns in one pocket - I said my money was all in silver: he then said he had changed the silver he took from me into gold - we continued to search, and found 20s. in silver in one of his trousers pockets, and 21s. 6d. in the other; he said it was mine, and begged for mercy.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Before he said any thing about the money, had you not said it would be better for him to confess, and you would give him a sovereign into the bargain? A. I did; he could have concealed the money if he liked; he bore an honest, industrious character.
COURT. Q. When was it you said he had better confess? A. The evening that I missed the money; not a word was said to him in the morning.
DANIEL DUTCH. I am an officer. I was sent for on the 1st of April, about eight o'clock in the morning, to the prosecutor's house, and found the prisoner there; I searched him, and found 9s. 5 3/4d. in his waistcoat pocket, three sovereigns in his right-hand breeches pocket, and 2l. in silver in his other pockets; I said I understood the money was all in silver; he then said, "Yes, it was so; pray forgive me, Mr. White, it was all your money; I have changed 3l. in silver for sovereigns:" no promise or inducement was held out to him in my presence - I found four shillings more on him than was lost.
JURY to JOHN WHITE. Q. Was the drawer locked? A. No; he had been out for a quarter of an hour or twenty minutes.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-234]
Before Mr. Justice Littledale.
