Before Mr. Justice Gazelee.
914. ELEANOR FOX and MARY STEEL were indicted for feloniously assaulting Edward McQuillen, on the King's highway, on the 31st of March, at St. Giles in the Fields, putting him in fear, and taking from his person, and against his will, 1 tooth-pick, value 2d.; 1 button value 1/4d.; 1 penny-piece, and 1 half-penny, his property.
EDWARD McQUILLEN. I live at No. 20, Buckeridge-street, St. Giles'. On the 31st of March I was in a public-house at the corner of Plumtree-street, and saw the prisoner Fox there; I came out of the house between eightand ten o'clock - I had been drinking, and cannot state the time I came out; two women followed me - Fox was one; Fox asked me to go home with her; I told her I would not - that I lodged convenient: and when I turned round the corner into the street I lived in, the woman who was with her put her hand into my pocket; I sang out for the watchman - I do not know what she took from my pocket; Fagan the officer came up immediately: I had been drinking for two days together - I was nearly drunk when this happened.
FRANCIS FAGAN. I am a patrol of Bow-street. On the 31st of March I was on duty in Buckeridge-street, St. Giles', I heard a cry of Watch! between ten and eleven o'clock at night, and saw Fox with her arms round the prosecutor's body; she stood behind him, with her arms clasped round his body, holding him behind - Steel was in front of him, rifling his pockets: I did not think him drunk - there was not much difference in him than there is now; I took hold of Fox, while my partner took Steel; I searched Fox, but found nothing on her: when I took hold of her a button dropped from her - I searched Steel, and observed she had something in her month: she denied that she had - I insisted on her opening her mouth, and shewing me what she had; she refused to do so, and I used force; after struggling with her for a quarter of an hour, I succeeded in getting from her mouth a tooth-pick and three halfpence farthing: I secured, and took them to the watch-house - the tooth-pick is here; Wheatley found a duplicate in her hand.
WILLIAM WHEATLEY. I was with Fagan, and saw the prisoners together, round the prosecutor, at the corner of George-street; I took Fox, and found a duplicate in Steel's right hand, and a piece of tobacco; the prosecutor said they were his, in the prisoner's presence.
DANIEL RIERDAN. I was with Fagan and Wheatley. I picked up a button which laid on the ground, between Steel and Fox; I had seen Steel throw something away, but cannot say whether it was a button; I thought it was money - it sounded like a shilling; she threw it away directly we came up - we found no money, as the crowd collected and the street was muddy; the button was one of a Chelsea pensioner's; I have had it ever since.
EDWARD NEYLON. I am a copper-plate printer. As I was going home I saw the prisoners going along with this man; I stopped and looked after them - I saw Steel following the prosecutor; I saw Steel with her left hand round the prosecutor's body, and her right hand in his trousers pocket; Fox followed behind at a distance - I afterwards saw them in Fagan's custody.
EDWARD MCQUILLEN. This tooth-pick belongs to me; I have had it two months - I know it by a crack on one side of it, and two marks on each end; I had it in my pocket all day, and had it in my hand several times: this duplicate is mine - it is for two silk handkerchiefs, pawned that day for 2s.; I had a button similar to this in my pocket: it came off this coat: I bought this coat of a Chelsea pensioner - I do not know what money I lost; I had the change of shilling out of three or four quarterns of gin.
Prisoner STEEL. Q. Did you not want to go home with me that night, and I said you should not? A. I do not recollect speaking to her at all.
FOX's Defence. The man knows I never touched him; this woman asked me to go and have a drop of drink - he followed us out of the house, insisted on going home with her, and he gave her a shove.
STEEL's Defence. I am totally innocent.
FOX - GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 20.
STEEL - GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 29.
[trialAccount t18290409-236]
Before Mr. Justice Littledale.
915. THOMAS QUINLY and CHARLOTTE JONES were indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of James Steel, on the 13th of January, and stealing therein 30 sovereigns, one 50l., and one 10l. Bank-note, his property.
MR. BARRY conducted the prosecution.
JAMES STEEL. I am a fellowship-porter, and live at No. 9. Wellington-street; at the time in question I lived in Billet-court, and rented the whole house, which had only two rooms. I left home at six o'clock in the morning of the 13th of January, returned at nine in the evening, and missed this property; I went into the bedroom, and found a hole in the ceiling large enough for persons to come through, and the tiles taken off.
ELIZABETH STEEL. I am the wife of James Steel. On the morning of the 13th of January I went out at eleven o'clock, and at half-past twelve I went up stairs, saw a hole in the ceiling, and the tiles taken off; the mattress was drawn from under the bed, and this money taken out of it; I had felt it there at nine that morning - there were thirty-four sovereigns, a 50l. and 10l. Bank note, which we received, as a compensation for a house, from the Dock Company; I do not know the number of the notes, nor the dates - the mattress was ripped open; I missed nothing else: the male prisoner lived next door, with Jones; I saw Quinly the same day, about two hours after the robbery; he came to his door; I did not speak to him - I was only absent half an hour; the prisoners have been married since the robbery; the man said he worked in a rope-ground - he lived at his mother's house, where I saw him; I have not seen him with any money since.
HANNAH JORDAN. I am the wife of Richard Jordan, a travelling-musician, and live in Tower-street, Westminster-road. I know the prisoner Jones; I saw her after Christmas - I do not know the month; it was six or seven weeks before I heard of this robbery: she called at my mother's house, where I was, and wanted to know if my mother knew where her father lived, and asked if she bought a few things, if she might leave them there; she gave her permission - I went out with her, and bought a little box and a Britannia metal tea-pot; we came home, and she left us - I saw her again about a week afterwards: she then asked my mother if she might bring her young man in - my mother gave her leave, and she brought the prisoner in- they drank tea at our house; they both called again next morning, and said they wished my mother was well enough to go out and purchase a few things for them; my mother was too ill, and I went: they bought a bedstead, mattress, chairs, wash-hand stand, and several small things, which came to about 4l. 16s.; they took a room next door to my mother's, and the things were sent home: I heard them order them to be sent there. I saw Quinly with four or five sovereigns, and I saw him hold up two notes - he told me they were sailor's notes; my mother said she thought noteswould not go; he said, "Oh, yes, I can get rid of them" -I looked over his shoulder, and saw one with "10" marked on it, and the other with "50;" he took two or three sovereigns out of a small bag - I believe it was all gold; I could not tell how much was in it: they went to another shop, and bought a bed and bedding.
COURT. Q. Did you know what sort of notes they were? A. No, they were too far from me.
Cross-examined by MR. CRESWELL. Q. Did he take them out and show them to you? A. No, I stood behind him at a distance - he took them out and held them up.
JANE HEWITT. I am the wife of Mark Hewitt, and live in Duke-street, Borough. Jones called on me one day after Christmas, when I was ill in bed; I think it was in January - she said she was going to be married to the young man, whose mother she worked with, and asked if I would let her leave some things at my house which she was going to buy; she left a box and a tea-pot - she locked the box, and went away; they both came eight or nine days after, and took some money out of the box - I saw something in their hand, in a little bag, and heard money rattle between them; I saw Quinly with two bits of dirty paper in his hand; he said they were notes - I said that notes did not pass - he said he could make those notes go any where, but they were of no use to any one else; they looked to me like dirty paper, and as he said he had saved his money at sea, I thought he was a sailor - they went to live next door, but moved before they were taken.
CHARLOTTE HEWITT. I am the daughter of Jane Hewitt. I was present when the prisoner Jones came; it was not long after Christmas; she came to know where her father lived; my mother did not know - my sister went out with her, and they brought home a box; she took the key of the box, and went away.
JAMES STEEL. The notes were clean when I had them - I got them from Barclay's banking-house; I kept my money in a dark short bag.
NOT GUILTY.
Second London Jury - before Mr. Recorder.
[trialAccount t18290409-237]
916. GEORGE COCKLIN and SAMUEL GOODWIN were indicted for stealing, on the 3d of March, 2 umbrellas, value 10s., the goods of John Teesdale.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
WILLIAM WADE. I am servant to Mr. John Teesdale, a solicitor of Fenchurch-street. There was a stand of umbrellas in the hall; on the morning of the 3d of March, between seven and eight o'clock, I passed through the hall, and the umbrellas were safe; there was a silk and a cotton one - the dustmen were there, taking away the dust as I went out; I came back about a quarter before eight, and the two umbrellas were gone - I have not seen them since.
WILLIAM LONG. On the morning of the 3d of March I was in Fenchurch-street, about a quarter before eight, o'clock; I have seen the prisoners about the street before that; I know them by sight - on the 3d of March I saw them near Mr. Teesdale's door at a quarter before eight; they were looking at the houses, as if looking for a number- the dust-cart was before the door; they were talking together - Goodwin went into Mr. Teesdale's house; Cocklin looked all about, and then whistled; Goodwin then came out with two umbrellas under his arm - they walked down London-street; I turned back and told the dustman - I saw nothing more of them; London-street would take them to Crutched-friars - I am quite sure they are the two men; I afterwards went up to George-yard, Wentworth-street, to the George public-house, to show the officers where I thought they would be - I saw them there; I did not go in; I saw them standing at the door - I told the officers, who went in and took them - but they were obstinate, and would not go with them; I am quite sure the prisoners are the persons; the officers were obliged to let go of them - a young woman was there, and they sent out a man to call in assistance; I saw them again next day in George-yard, and knew them to be the men - they said nothing to me; they were taken before the Justice about a fortnight afterwards, and I was certain of them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How old are you? A. Thirteen years; the officers could not take them- they forced through the house; I was never taken up for any thing - I gave information; they were followed immediately to their lodgings - no umbrellas were found; I lost sight of them, because I turned round to tell the dustman; one went down London-street, and the other down Mark-lane; that took about five minutes - the public-house is a quarter of a mile off.
Q. Did you tell the officers who the men were? A. I told them where I thought they would be, because I had often seen them about George-yard; I live in George-street - I work for Mr. Smith - I was going over to my grandmother's, in the Borough; I did not follow down London-street: I was never in custody myself.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. What is your father? A. A labourer at St Katharine's Docks. I get work where I can- it was three quarters of an hour after that I saw them in George-yard.
JAMES DRURY. I am a cellar-man to Lee and Bradshaw, of Mineing-lane. On the 3d of March, between eight and nine o'clock in the morning. I was passing by Mr. Teesdale's door, and saw the two prisoners talking together at the door; I watched them - on my looking at them, Goodwin walked round Mark-lane, and pretended to look at a door opposite for a number; he then came back, spoke to the other, walked into the passage, and came out - I only saw the top of one umbrella; he spoke to Cocklin, and walked round London-street - I went after him with a permit-writer, and near the steps leading to Crutched-friars, I called out "Stop him with the umbrella;" he then began to run, and turned down the steps into Crutched-friars - I ran into the middle of the road, calling out Stop him! I saw Devey running after him; I then left them.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Did you see Hewitt? A. No; I was on the other side of the way - I was about twelve yards from the door, when Goodwin went in; there might be another person on the other side of the cart without my seeing him; Goodwin went by the back of the cart, I followed on the opposite; I saw no boy about, but I was noticing the prisoners; I knew Goodwin before - I saw him afterwards at Lambeth-street; I said he was the person - I saw but one umbrella in his hand; when I followed him, he put it down, and held it down - he might have had two.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. You saw him near the house,then went across the way, so that the cart was between you? A. Yes; he spoke to the other, and immediately walked into the passage - a whistle could have been given without my hearing it; he walked quietly till I called Stop him! and then ran.
THOMAS DEVEY. I am street-keeper of Aldgate. On the morning of the 3d of March I was on duty in Crutched-friars, and pursued Goodwin, whom Drury pointed out - he was running with two umbrellas under his arm, one green and the other a dark colour; he turned through Northumberland-alley, ran across Fenchurch-street into Leadenhall-street, and at last got away; I went back afterwards and gave information - I afterwards went to Mr. Teesdale's house and saw Long; I went with him to George-yard - both the prisoners were drinking at the bar; I went and seized Goodwin by the collar, and told him he had committed the robbery; the patrol seized Cocklin - a dreadful struggle ensued: they called for assistance from a person, I think, named White - there were four or five at the bar; I pulled Goodwin out of the house, the patrol had got Cocklin inside, and they were all upon him - I went to his assistance, and they all retreated out of the back door - I went to Lambeth-street Office and gave information.
Cross-examined. Q. He had no umbrella at the public-house? A. No.
WILLIAM ARTHUR. I am inspector of the watch. I accompanied Devey to the George, George-yard - Long said, "There they are;" Devey took hold of Goodwin and I of Cocklin - he screamed out that I was going to grab him, which means to take him; a woman came to his assistance, and threw herself between us; he broke from me, and ran through the back door of the house - I followed him - three people came to his assistance and rescued him from me a second time; I lost him, and did not see him again till the 17th of March; I knew him again.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You seized him without saying a word? A. I had my staff in my hand, and said, "I wanted him;" he called out that I was going to grab him; that could be heard outside the house; I did not know Long before, and never knew any thing against him.
THOMAS SHELLSWELL. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. On the 11th of March I went to a lodging-house in Wentworth-street, and found the two prisoners in the same room; I told them I came to apprehend them for a crack down the road - that means housebreaking; Cocklin said, "If that I the case we will go with you quietly; but if it is for the City job we are sure to be lugged;" which means transported - they went quietly.
Cross-examined. Q. A crack means breaking open a house, not going into an open door? A. No; it means a regular burglary: I did not take them at their regular lodgings, they were out of the way; I knew their lodgings, and searched them, but found nothing.
JURY to LONG. Q. Did you know any thing of the prisoners before to induce you to watch them? A. No; I watched them because I thought they were after no good; I had seen them about; they had been pointed out to me; I knew they lived in Cage-street, Wentworth-street.
GOODWIN'S Defence. I went up to Cocklin's house, and had not been there five minutes before Shellswell came in.
COCKLIN's Defence. I never was there.
COCKLIN - GUILTY. Aged 20.
GOODWIN - GUILTY. Aged 22.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-238]
917. SARAH BOWICK was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of February, at St. Botolph Without, Aldgate, 1 watch, value 10l.; 1 watch-chain, value 5l.; 2 seals, value 20s.; 2 rings, value 15s.; 1 watch-key, value 10s.; 1 box, value 5s.; and 1 slide, value 15s., the goods of James Muirhead, in the dwelling-house of John Little .
HANNAH MUIRHEAD. I am the wife of James Muirhead - we live at the Crown and Magpie tavern - it is my father's house - his name is John Little - it is in the parish of St. Botolph, Aldgate, and in the City. The prisoner was servant in the house, and had been so about ten days or a fortnight at the time of the robbery - there was one more servant in the house besides her; I lost a gold watch, with a chain to it - it was locked up in my bed-room, and had two seals, two rings, one key, a box and slide - they were all locked in my bed-room, which is one of the rooms detached from the house, but part of the dwelling-house; the prisoner used to clean the room, and attend to it; I know the property was safe the day before I missed it - I saw it all, it was locked in a drawer - I put the key into another drawer, which was not locked; I missed it next day, and made inquiry about it, and the prisoner helped me to look for it - but it was not found; I saw the watch at Lambeth-street office, and the chain, but it was not then attached to the watch; when I lost it, the watch-chain, seals, a ring, key, and slide, were all fastened together, and put in a red morocco case - I saw it at Lambeth-street about a month after I missed it, which was on the 2d of February; my father rented the house - my husband was at sea.
WILLIAM BROCK. I am shopman to Nicholls and Latter, pawnbrokers, No. 80, Broad-street, Ratcliff-cross. I took a gold chain in pawn on the 16th of February for 24s. of the prisoner - I am certain she is the person - she never pawned there before; she was about a quarter of an hour with me - I am quite certain of her person: the prisoner said the chain belonged to a Mrs. Page, for whom she pawned it, and her own name was Sarah Bowick, No. 2, Webb's-place, which is in Limehouse; I produced it at the office about a fortnight after - Mrs. Muirhead claimed it.
MRS. MUIRHEAD. This is my chain - I had had it about three months - I am positive of it.
HENRY HAWKINS. I am a pawnbroker, of Catherine-street, Limehouse. I bought a seal of the prisoner on the 16th or 17th of February; I am certain of her - she gave me her own name; she said it was given her by a former sweetheart, and having married another person, she did not want it; the officer has it - this is it (looking at it) - I gave it to the officer - I gave 8s. for it.
MRS. MUIRHEAD. I know this seal - it is exactly like mine - it is an amethyst seal, and no engraving on it; the prisoner left the service exactly a fortnight after I lost it.
EDWARD WILLIAM WIGGINS. I am servant to Mr. Dicker, a pawnbroker, of Three Colt-street, Limehouse. I have a gold watch, pawned on the 21st of February, by the prisoner, in the name of Sarah Page; she said shepawned it for her mother; I am quite sure of her person- I have seen her in the neighbourhood before - she was about twenty minutes with me; I advanced 4l. on it; it was produced at the office on the 3d of March, and claimed by Mrs. Muirhead.
MRS. MUIRHEAD. I am certain it is mine - my husband has no other Christian name; I have been married about twelve months; I had had the watch about three months - and am certain of it.
JAMES LEA. I am an officer of Lambeth-street. I apprehended the prisoner first on the 23d of February, in the room of Mrs. Higgins, a witness who is not here; I told her it was on suspicion of stealing a gold watch; she denied all knowledge of it; in coming down stairs she whipped very quick into the first floor room - I went to look for her, and found her behind the door; I found nothing on her; I took her to the office, and she was discharged: about four days after, Mrs. Little, Mrs. Muirhead's mother, brought her to the office in a coach; I then went round to the different pawnbrokers - I searched her, but found no duplicates on her; I have a seal, pawned in Ratcliff-highway; I have also a slide, ring, and key, which were found by the witness who is not here.
MRS. MUIRHEAD. This is one of my seals; this slide, key, and ring were all attached to my watch - I know them by the pattern; I went after the prisoner's character, and was satisfied with it.
JOHN LITTLE. I keep this tavern, and rent the whole premises; my daughter slept in a room within the premises - it was part of the dwelling-house that I rented - it was all rated as one house - my daughter paid me no rent - I could turn her out of the room whenever I pleased.
GUILTY - DEATH. Aged 22.
Recommended to mercy by the prosecutrix, believing it to be her first offence, and on account of her youth.
[trialAccount t18290409-239]
918. THOMAS DANIELS and CAROLINE KENSETT were indicted for stealing, on the 5th of March, 5 handkerchiefs, value 8s. 9d., the goods of John Woolmer.
THOMAS WARWICK. I am a shopman to John Woolmer, a linen-draper and haberdasher, of Barbican. These handkerchiefs were all in one piece - about the 5th of March I had been showing some to a gentleman, and this one among them - I saw them safe a quarter of an hour before - the prisoners came into the shop together to look at some cotton handkerchief, which were in a different part of the shop, within reach of these handkerchiefs - they were on the counter; they left the shop without buying; I did not miss the handkerchiefs till an officer gave me information; he had taken the prisoners, and produced the handkerchiefs, with my own mark on them.
GEORGE BULLEN. I am a coach-maker. I was near Woolmer's shop, about seven o'clock in the evening; I first saw a lad looking in at the window - in a few minutes after, the prisoners came up and joined him - they stood at the window about ten minutes; the lad went into the shop first, and the prisoners followed him; I saw them looking at the cotton handkerchiefs, and then went to the end of the shop - I saw them all three come out together; they turned up Golden-lane, which is about two doors from the shop - a waggon passed, and I called to Vann, the officer, to follow them; they walked very fast- I ran to keep them in sight; I never lost sight of them; they turned up Playhouse-yard; the lad and the female went up the court, and the man stood at the corner - they returned down again, and Vann took bold of the woman; I took the man, the boy ran away; Vann said to the woman "What have you here?" she said Nothing, and he took the piece of handkerchiefs from under her shawl: it was claimed by Warwick - they were searched, and 3 1/2d. found on them together.
JOHN VANN. I had been watching the prisoners for half an hour, and saw them go into the shop and come out - I laid hold of the female at the end of the court; the lad went away - I did not observe him with them at that time; seeing the woman's bosom bulky, I put my hand under her shawl and found the handkerchiefs; she said she had picked them up on the pavement - she had 3 1/2d.; Daniels had no money.(Property produced and sworn to.)
KENSETT's Defence. The other prisoner is innocent.
DANIELS' Defence. I know nothing about the concern; when I came out of the shop I was going home, and did not positively know any thing was taken.
DANIELS - GUILTY. Aged 21.
KENSETT - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-240]
919. HENRY JOHNS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 3s., the goods of John White Welch, from his person.
JOHN WHITE WELCH. I am clerk to a merchant, and live at Camberwell. On the 17th of March, about ten o'clock in the morning, I was in Gracechurch-street; I had come from Camberwell, and had a yellow silk handkerchief in my pocket - I felt my coat move, instantly turned round, and observed the prisoner, and a young man in company with him; I laid hold of the prisoner by the hand, in which I found my handkerchief - he was in the act of putting it under his coat: a gentleman who was with me seized his companion - we took them both to the Mansion-house; the prisoner said he had picked it up, but I felt it go from my pocket, turned round, and caught it in his hand - his companion was discharged - I have not seen him since; the handkerchief had my name on it in full length, and the name of my cousin, which was marked on it as well, by mistake.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Had you taken the handkerchief out shortly before? A. Not that I recollect; I felt it go out of my pocket, which made me turn round - he said he had picked it up; I saw nobody run away.
JAMES BOOTH. I was with Mr. Welch, in Gracechurch-street; he turned round very sharp, and I turned round and caught the prisoner's hand in his own breastpocket with the handkerchief, trying to hide it there - I laid hold of the other.
THOMAS HERDSFIELD. I am a constable. The prisoner was delivered into my charge at the Mansion-house.
Cross-examined. Q. Do you know the one who hasbeen released? A. Yes; he is a notorious character, and older than the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going to the Borough to look for work; this gentleman's handkerchief was on the ground; I immediately stooped to pick it up, and put it into my pocket - he turned round and I offered it to him; he took me to the Mansion-house; the other man, who was a stranger to me, has been convicted three times.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Confined Three Months.

920. PATRICK CALLAGHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of April, 3lbs. weight of pork, value 1s. 6d., the goods of William Gammage.
WILLIAM GAMMAGE. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Drury-lane. On the 1st of April, between seven and eight o'clock in the evening, the witness gave me information, and I missed this pork; I had seen it safe within half an hour, in my window, which was open.
WILLIAM MACKEY. I saw the prisoner stand at the window, and take out this pork; I gave an alarm, and Deverell went and took him with it - he did not seem so drunk when he took it, as when he was brought back.
WILLIAM DEVERELL. Mackey gave an alarm; I pursursued and took the prisoner; he directly offered to pay for the pork - it was then lying very near him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was in liquor.
GUILTY. Aged 37.

921. ADOLPHUS DICKSON, EDWARD BROWN, and CHARLES BRYANT were indicted for stealing, on the 24th of March, 2 shirts, value 10s.; 3 collars, value 1s. 6d., and 1 stock, value 6d., the goods of John Eates.
ELIZA EATES. I am the wife of John Eates. I went, on the 24th of March, from a lady's, in Baker-street, to whom these things belonged, to my mother's, in Bagnigge-wells-road; I left these articles there, tied up in a white handkerchief, by the side of the counter - I went out for about five minutes, and they were then gone; these are the things; here are two shirts, three collars, and one stock.
ELIZABETH EATES. I am Eates' mother-in-law. She left a bundle at my shop on the 24th of March; she went out; Brown and Bryant came in, asked for half a pint of beer, and change for a shilling; I gave them the change - while I stooped to draw the beer, they were gone, and the bundle likewise; I knew Dickson, but I did not see him at that time - he was about the door, I believe, but I did not exactly notice; Dickson was not in the shop.
WILLIAM ROBERTS. I am a constable. I took Dickson on the 24th of March, about half-past nine o'clock at night: I asked if he knew any thing about a robbery of some linen; he said No; I told him to get up, and go with me - he then said the things should be forthcoming in the morning; I asked where they were; he said at Mr. Button's; I asked where the tickets were; he said they did not give any; I locked him up, went back, and found this shirt under the head of his bed.
SAMUEL MOUNTSTEPHENS. I took up Brown and Bryant- they denied all knowledge of the things.
EDWARD HADDAWAY. Dickson desired me one night to pawn a shirt, a stock, a handkerchief, and a collar - I do not know when it was; I pawned them at Mr. Button's - they were in a bundle; I was desired to sell the ticket; and went to do so, but it was stopped.
JOHN BUTTON. I am servant to Daniel Button, a pawnbroker. On the 24th of March I received these articles from Haddaway.
HENRY DEYEN. On the 24th of March I was at the back of the field, and saw Dickson run in a contrary way from the prosecutor's shop, with a bundle; he was on the other side of the bridge, a good step from the shop.
Dickson put in a written defence, stating that he had found the property in question. He received a good character.
DICKSON - GUILTY. Aged 17.
BROWN - GUILTY. Aged 16.
BRYANT - GUILTY. Aged 16.
Whipped and Discharged.

923. WILLIAM HOGG and WILLIAM BANKS were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of March, 1 rug, value 6s., and 1 umbrella, value 2s., the goods of the Honourable Richard Hobart Fitzgibbon.
ELIJAH ASHLEY. I am a hackney-coach proprietor. I was in Sloane-street, New-road, on the 18th of March, about a quarter-past nine o'clock; I then went to the Haymarket; I there saw Banks, who is a hackney-coachman - Hogg was with him; I saw Hogg take a rug from a gentleman's carriage, which was going by empty towards the House of Commons, and put it into Bank's coach, who was feeding his horses at the time; I do not know whether Bank saw it.
Cross-examined by MR. CLARKSON. Q. Does not Banks keep a coach of his own? A. Yes.
HENRY HOWLETT. I am a watchman. I was on dutyon the evening of the 18th of March; Ashley gave me information, and I took Hogg - I then opened Banks' carriage, and took this rug and umbrella out; Banks said he knew nothing of them, or of Hogg.
HENRY WILLIAMS. I am a patrol. I assisted in taking Hogg - I saw Howlett take these things from the chariot; Hogg attempted to escape from me - I said if he did I would draw my sword; Banks said he knew nothing of them, or of the prisoner, but he said afterwards that he lived next door to him - Colonel Fitzgibbon's servant swore to these things, but he is not here.
NOT GUILTY.

EDWARD WILLSON. I am shopman to Mr. Francis Moor, of St. Martin's-court. On the 9th of March I missed an umbrella from the shop, which I had seen not long before; the officer brought in the prisoner with it.
JOHN GROOM. I am an officer. On the 9th of March, about nine o'clock in the evening. I observed the prisoner and another lad loitering about this shop; I watched them, and saw the prisoner take something from his pocket - he immediately stepped into the shop, and brought out this umbrella; he came down the court with the other -I tripped up the heels of the other, and took the prisoner with this umbrella and knife.(Property produced and sworn to.)

926. DAVID LEWIS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 1 pair of boots, value 5s., the goods of Samuel Swoolston.
JOSEPH HOTCHKISS. I am shopman to Mr. Samuel Swoolston, a boot-maker. On the 14th of March a young man told me these boots were gone, and I missed them from the nail I had seen them on but a few minutes before, when I was at the door - I went across; I saw the prisoner at the length of the street, but could not go after him - they have not been found.
JOSEPH CHURCH. I was coming past the door at a quarter after ten o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner stoop and creep under the front of the window - he put his hand and took the boots off a nail in the shop; he ran into High-street, Bloomsbury - I went to the house where he delivered them, and should have had them, but there were so many ruffians about; I swear I saw him take them - he ran to what is called Rats' Castle.
THOMAS PLUME. I took the prisoner on the 14th of March - I found nothing on him; he said the boots were in Rats' Castle, but said another boy took them.
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking past the shop - another boy stooped and took the boots; the gentleman knocked me right under the horse's feet - they afterwards came and took me: I said I knew nothing about them -I ran home; they came again and took me.
JOSEPH CHURCH. There was no other boy with him, nor any one near enough to take them but him; I could not catch him, he ran so fast.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Whipped and Discharged.

927. GEORGE OAKLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of March, 6 lbs. weight of pork, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Seth Barber.
SETH BARBER I am a cheesemonger, and live in Baldwin's-gardens. On the 21st of March I heard a call of Stop thief! and ran out; Flemming brought the prisoner and this pork in - I knew it to be mine, and had seen it about ten minutes before.
JOHN FLEMMING. On that evening, between eight and nine o'clock, I was passing the prosecutor's shop; I saw the prisoner snatch the piece of pork, put it in his apron, and run off - I pursued; he was stopped in Kirby-street, Hatton-garden - I am quite sure he is the person.
JOSEPH COGGER. I am a publican. I found this piece of pork in my passage, which is about one hundred yards from the prosecutor's; I did not see who put it there - several persons ran by the house.
MATTHIAS WELDHEN. I am an officer. I heard a cry of Stop thief! I saw a person run from the prosecutor's shop, across Leather-lane; I secured him in Kirby-street- I did not see at first who it was; the prisoner said he did it, because he was in distress, and had nothing to eat.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Fourteen Days.

Before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
928. WILLIAM POPE was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 1 counterpane, value 12s.; 1 pair of blankets, value 20s.; 1 sheet, value 6s.; 1 pillow, value 7s., and 1 bolster, value 10s., the goods of William Leach.
ELIZABETH LEACH. I am the wife of William Leach; we live in Zion-terrace, and let a furnished lodging to the prisoner on the 18th of February by the week; all the articles stated were in the room, as part of the furniture - he went away on the 20th without giving notice, and these articles were missing; we have not found any of them - he was taken in about a month.
THOMAS BINFORD KERSWELL. I am an officer. Leach gave me a description of the prisoner, and I took him in St. James'-park.
Prisoner. I throw myself on the mercy of the Court.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-250]
929 LYDIA SHEPHERD was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 sovereign and 1 sixpence, the monies of Thomas John Freeman, from his person.
THOMAS JOHN FREEMAN. I keep a coal-shed in New Gravel-lane. On the 16th of March I was at the Ship and Unicorn public-house in New Gravel-lane - I found the prisoner there; it was between one and two o'clock - I was sober; there were but a couple of pots of beer between four or five of us - the prisoner lived within two doors of me; I have sold her coals, and when I went in she said, "What, are you come you old b-r?" I said,"You had better use better words and pay me for my coals;" for she owed me 8d. - she said, "I will have your life before I will pay you;" as I was sitting against the window they put some flour all over my clothes, and threw some in my eyes, then threw me backwards, throttled, and robbed me of a sovereign and a sixpence - the prisoner was kneeling on my breast, and was choking me; I felt her hand in my pocket - I could not call out, because she strangled me; there were a good many persons there in the same line - she keeps a bad house; there were two or three men there - they went and told my wife, who came, caught hold of her, and broke her hold: I did not go to tell any officer - next morning my eyes were so bad that I could not get up; before I got better she went and got a warrant for me, and when I told my tale they committed her.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How many people were in the room when she choked and robbed you? A. Five, six or seven - there was not a single man besides myself; two men went and told my wife, but they did not see any part of the row - after they went out of the house I did not knock the prisoner down, I am sure; I could not go that night to an officer or a Magistrate - I could not see out of my eyes; I did keep a house, but I had left it that day - I have had two lodgers, but I never took in lodgers by the hour, nor by the day; I have by the week, never by the night - I have been six or seven months in the potatoe business; I cannot tell whether I called for a glass of gin in the house - I never drank one; if I had asked for ten glasses of gin I could have had them - I have understood the prisoner's husband has died since this happened.
SARAH GINGLE. I went to the house, saw the prosecutor on the floor, and the prisoner on his breast - she had her right hand in his right-hand waistcoat pocket, and said"You old b-r, before I have done, I will have your life;" I came out directly - I saw there was white-wash or colour on his back, and all about him; I afterwards saw his wife take him home.
Cross-examined. Q. Is not the prosecutor your father? A. Yes; the report was given by the fiddler and plasterer- I ran over; I did not notice my father's eyes - I did not see him for two or three days afterwards.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-251]
930. CHARLES LEDGERWOOD and ABEL SAMELLS were indicted for stealing, on the 19th of February, 1 bushel of coals, value 1s. 6d., the goods of Job Cook.
JOB COOK. I am in the coal trade, and live at Hammersmith. On the 19th of February, a barge had come in with twenty-one chaldron of coals, about five o'clock in the afternoon; I sent a person to watch it, as it was to remain at the wharf all night.
WILLIAM HEDGES. I was sent to watch the barge: I was playing with a white dog between seven and eight o'clock, and saw the two prisoners come on the barge; I told them they had better get off, or it would be worse for them: they would not - but they threw down the coals to two others, and they ran off with them - I ran after them; Ledgerwood dropped his piece of coal - the others got away with theirs; I knew the two prisoners before.
Prisoner SAMELLS. He said he never saw me before - but a bricklayer's boy told him it was me. Witness. I know him very well, and knew him at the time.
JAMES SMITH. I am a constable. I took up the prisoners, and accused them of stealing the coals - and as I was bringing them to town, they acknowledged taking a few; they were very nearly rescued from me.
LEDGERWOOD's Defence. He took my brother - I went to the cage to see him, and this boy swore to me - the officer then came and said, "I want you for the coals;" I walked with him - he had no trouble with me.
SAMELL's Defence. We have had seven weeks in Clerkenwell prison - and, if we were guilty, I think it is quite punishment enough - I know no more of them than a new-born baby.
LEDGERWOOD - GUILTY. Aged 17.
Transported for Seven Years.
SAMELLS - GUILTY. Aged 15.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18290409-252]
931. WILLIAM SAUNDERS was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of April, 2 pairs of shoes, value 3s., the goods of Robert George Brodie.
ROBERT GEORGE BRODIE. I am a ladies' shoemaker. and live in Cranbourn-passage. About a quarter after three o'clock, on the 6th of April, I was going over the way to a person who conducted my business during my illness, and the prisoner took these shoes from the corner bar, but I did not see him; they have not been found.
JOSEPH BURKE. I live on the first floor of a house opposite the prosecutor's. About a quarter past three o'clock on the 6th of April, I was looking through my window, and saw the prisoner looking in the prosecutor's window - he went to the door, and unhooked the two pairs of shoes - before I could open the window he was gone; I went down, and saw the prosecutor; we went to Castle-street, and saw the prisoner loitering about, as if he was coming back again; I do not know what became of the shoes; I am sure that he is the lad - he was taken in three or four minutes.
GUILTY. Aged 17.
Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18290409-253]
932. MARY TOOLE was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of March, 2 tongues, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Sculling.
SARAH KENDRICK. On the 7th of March I was at the shop of Mr. John Sculling, a cheesemonger; the prisoner came in, and called for a quarter of a lb. of butter - shepaid 2d. for it: she went to the tub, and took out one tongue - she turned again, and put another in her apron; she was going out, and the lad asked if she had paid for them; she made no answer, but struck him, and struck me, and tore my bonnet off; the watchman was called, and took her.
ARTHUR POWER. I serve in the shop; I saw the two tongues in the prisoner's apron - to the best of my knowledge she had not bought them; she struck me because I stopped her; there was a shopman serving.
JOHN SCULLING. These tongues were mine; I was outside the window; when I took hold of the prisoner, she fell down - I left her. while I got the watchman.
WILLIAM HORNSBY. I am the watchman. I took the prisoner - she was in liquor, but seemed to know what she was about, and was capable of walking.
Prisoner's Defence. I had bought the tongues, and then went with a friend and had something to drink; as I was going home, I called for some butter, and was going to take the tongues, for which I had paid the shopman.
GUILTY. Aged 23.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Six Weeks.

No evidence. NOT GUILTY.

923. THOMAS WILLIAMS was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 1 looking-glass, value 16s., the goods of William Smith.
WILLIAM SMITH. I live in York-street, Westminster. I lost this glass on Saturday evening, the 11th of April, about ten minutes before seven o'clock; it had been about four feet inside the front of my shop.
THOMAS TRENT. I am in the Coldstream Guards. I saw the prisoner reach into the shop and take this glass - I took him and brought him back - there was another young man with him who threw a sack over the glass when the prisoner put it down on the stones.
Prisoner's Defence. I had been to Pimlico and was returning - the witness came and said I had stolen the glass, but I had not seen it.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18290409-256]
934. JONAS SIMONS was indicted for stealing, on the 17th of February, 1 crown, and 1 half-crown, the monies of William Arnold Hadden Hows.
JOHN COOK. I am foreman to Mr. Hows, a pawnbroker, of High-street, St. Giles'. On the 17th of February the prisoner came into one of the private boxes, and asked the shopman to give him a sovereign for a pounds worth of silver; the shopman is not here; I saw him give him the sovereign - he did not seem satisfied, and said, as well as he could speak in broken English, that he wanted it changed for two half-sovereigns; he gave him the two half-sovereigns and then he did not seem satisfied, but wanted one of the half-sovereigns changed for silver; the shopman pulled out the till to do so; the till had been out before - it was just under the counter, opposite the private boxes; he then put the half-sovereign on the counter, put his hand into the till, and said in broken English, how easily any body might help themselves; I saw him draw his hand from the till, and put it down by the side of his trousers pocket; he then took his hand from his side, and put it into the till a second time; I had not seen him put any thing into his pocket, but he was saying something which we could not understand; I watched him more sharply - I was standing on his right-hand side, and saw between his thumb and the palm of his right hand a crown-piece, two half-crowns, and some other silver, which he drew from the till, and put into his right-hand breeches pocket; the shopman and I together accused him of it - he said something that we could not understand, and shifted his hand from his breeches pocket to the side pocket of his coat, or to that part of his person - his hand was under his coat on his left side: Mr. Hows sent for an officer and had him taken - this half-sovereign and the 10s. were laid on the counter during the scuffle.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. Have you always given the same account of this? A. Yes; I did not state the prisoner asked us to give him change for a sovereign; he wanted a sovereign for silver; he spoke English very imperfectly - we had some difficulty in understanding what he wanted; he pointed to the drawer, that he wanted 10s. for a half-sovereign; I saw the two half-crowns and the crown in his hand, and some more silver; I swear I saw shillings and sixpences in his hand - there were, I suppose, 5l., 6l., or 7l. in the till - it was not so very large; a female was on the same side of the counter as he was - he could see her if he looked round; she is not here, she has left her lodgings; the prisoner made great resistance; Mr. Hows jumped over the counter and seized him, and they were scuffling when I left; he put his hand across to show what sort of silver he wanted - the counter is about four feet high, and about two feet and a half wide - and the till was opposite him.
THOMAS WRIGHT. I was sent for, and found the prisoner and Mr. Hows scuffling together; I took the prisoner into the back parlour, and from his left-hand waistcoat pocket I took 2l. 13s. in silver - there was one crownpiece, six half-crowns, and shillings and sixpences; I began to feel at his trousers pocket, and from his left-hand, trousers pocket he drew a purse which contained money -I took it to Marlborough street, where Mr. Plank opened it - it contained thirty-six sovereigns and a half; I do not know why the woman is not here; I went to her lodgings several times, and last Saturday the landlady told me that a Jewess had been there and given her money.
Cross-examined. Q. Had not the prisoner a pack of goods near him? A. Yes, he had: I did not search his coat pockets till we got to Marlborough-street.
WILLIAM ARNOLD HADDEN HOWS. I am the owner of the shop. The prisoner wade great resistance, and wished to get away.
Cross-examined. Q. He is a foreigner - did you explain to him the object you had in view? A. I did not.
Prisoner's Defence. (Through an interpreter.) I am innocent; I did not want any thing of the kind - I had plenty of money.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-257]
934. JOHN RAWLINS and MARY ANN HAYTER were indicted for stealing, on the 14th of March, 2 sheets, value 5s.; 1 clock, value 10s.; 1 pair of bellows, value 1s.; 1 counterpane, value 5s.; 1 candlestick, value 6d.; 2 pillow-cases, value 1s.; 1 looking-glass, value 2s. 6d., and 1 flatiron, value 6d., the goods of Richard Lowe.
HAYTER pleaded GUILTY.
RICHARD LOWE. I live at No. 74, Turnmill-street. I let a lodging to the prisoners on the 23d or 24th of February - they left on the 14th of March without notice; I got into the room and missed the property stated; I had let them both out on the Saturday night with a bundle and a basket.
DANIEL PAINTER. I am an officer. I apprehended Hayter - the property has not been found.
RAWLINS' Defence. I know nothing of the robbery - this woman knows so; I took nothing out of the room.
RAWLINS - GUILTY. Aged 36.
936. JOHN RAWLINS and MARY ANN HAYTER were again indicted for stealing, on the 28th of March, 1 bed, value 2l.; 1 looking-glass, value 8s.; 1 bolster, value 3s.; 2 sheets, value 2s.; 1 counterpane, value 2s.; 1 pillow, value 2s.; 1 candlestick, value 1s.: 4 prints, framed, value 3s.; 1 pillow-case, value 6d.; 2 brass weights, value 2s., and 2 window-curtains, value 1s., the goods of William Clarke.
HAYTER pleaded GUILTY.
ELIZABETH CLARK. I am the wife of William Clark: we live in Great Sutton-street. Hayter came and took my lodging on the 25th of March - she asked if I would let her husband look at it; Rawlins stood opposite: she beckoned him up - he said, "My dear, do you like the room?" she said, "Yes - it is 4s. 6d. if I find sheets and 5s. if I don't;" they came in the same night, and were there all the week; they went in and out several times -Hayter came on the Saturday evening, about six o'clock, and said, "Is my husband here?" I said No; she said,"What a lucky house your's is - my husband has met with a person who paid him half a sovereign, and I am to have another presently, and will pay you;" the articles stated were my property, and are all lost.
Prisoner RAWLINS. Q. Do you say I was present looking at the lodging? A. Yes; I have heard you there as late as half-past ten o'clock - I never saw you go out with a bundle.
DANIEL PAINTER. I took the prisoners; these few feathers were left in the room.
RAWLINS' Defence. I know nothing at all of the property going any more than you do, who are about to sentence me.
RAWLINS - GUILTY. Aged 36.
Transported for Seven Years for each offence.
There was another indictment against the prisoners, which was not tried.
[trialAccount t18290409-258]
937. JOHN KEMP was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of February, 1 pair of shoes, value 5s., the goods of Edward Thomas Lands.
EDWARD THOMAS LANDS. I am a shoemaker, and live in Hackney-road. I know these things to be mine; the prisoner worked seven or eight months for me, and had the care of my shop, which was robbed - we suspected him, and had him taken; the duplicate of these shoes was found on him: I have lost a great deal of property; he had the key of the shop, and slept there.
JAMES HILL. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house, and I found on him the duplicate of a pair of shoes.
WILLIAM CAWOOD. I am a pawnbroker, and live in Hackney-road. On the 20th of February I took in these shoes, and gave this duplicate for them - I do not know to whom.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had pledged the shoes to purchase necessary articles for the business.
MR. LANDS. That is not true.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Confined Three Months.

941. HENRY BACON was indicted for stealing, on the 5th of April, 68lbs. weight of leaden pipe, value 10s., and 1 fixture, (i.e.) 1 brass cock, value 6d., the goods of Samuel Rivett, and fixed to a certain building of his.
WILLIAM SAXTON. I am a mattress-maker. I know the building of Mr. Rivett - it is No. 19, Wellington-street, Kingsland-road; it is a finished house, but not occupied. On the morning of the 5th of April, at a quarter-past two o'clock, I was going to bed, and heard an alarm; I opened my window, looked out, and an opposite neighbour said there were thieves in the next house - I went down, and heard the bolt of the parlour window drawn; three or four men came out of the house - I closely pursued the prisoner, who was one, and held him till the watchman came.
Prisoner. I was behind him all the way down the street; I never was in the window at all - I could not jump out. Witness. No, you were before me; I never lost sight of you - I said, "You are one of the party," and you said, "I am not."
JOSEPH ARNOLD. I am a watchman. The witness gave the prisoner to me; I went to the house after we came from the watch-house, and found this piece of pipe and a brass cock - they appeared to have been just cut off.
THOMAS FRANKLIN. I am a watchman. I went to the house, and found this pipe lying in the passage.
SAMUEL RIVETT. The house belongs to me, and was not occupied; I had not been there for four or five months - I had left the key next door, but the water came in on the Saturday, and it could not without this pipe; they could not have water till the pipe was put on again - the officer told me of this, and I went on the Sunday; the lead appeared fresh cut.
CHARLES WALLER. I am a Bow-street patrol. I went to the house, and found this pipe and cock; the lead is fresh cut, and part of it was lying near the main-pipe in the kitchen.
JOHN CRICK. I live next door to the house where the lead was cut. This spoon, which was found on the prisoner, is my property - it was in my back premises; they got over the wall to cut the pipe off; the key was left at No. 20, and the pipe was cut from there and No. 19.
Prisoner's Defence (written.) I was returning home, and passing by Wellington-street, I heard the cry of Stop thief! I went down to see what was the matter, and when I had got about half way down the street, I kicked something before me; I took it up, and found it was a spoon - I put it into my pocket, and followed after them; when I had got to the top of the street, on turning round the corner, I slipped down - the witness turned round, and laid hold of me.
GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-263]
942. EDWARD BUTLER was indicted for stealing, on the 2d of April, 6 half-crowns, 7 shillings 3 sixpences, and 9d. in copper, the monies of Francis Macnamara, from his person.
WILLIAM LEWIS. I was at the White Lion public-house, in St. Giles', on the evening of the 2d of April; the prisoner and prosecutor came in together - the prosecutor was very tipsy; I saw the prisoner put his hand into the prosecutor's left-hand pocket, and take out the money- it was in a bit of paper; he gave out 1s. to pay for the beer - he kept the money in his hand some time, and then put it into his own pocket; there were five or six people in the same box; there was no concealment - he got up, went into the yard, and came back; I told him if he did not give him the money I would fetch an officer - he told me not to trouble my head about it; I went for the officer, and the money was found on him: he was not so drunk as the prosecutor - he had been drinking.
FRANCIS MACNAMARA. I am a Chelsea pensioner. I had been with the prisoner, and we had each got our pensious; I got drunk, and do not know what happened.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18290409-264]
943. ROBERT BURN was indicted for stealing, on the 3d of April, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Parton, from his person.
JOHN PARTON. I am an attorney. I was in Charlotte-street, Fitzroy-square, on the 3d of April, and was informed the prisoner had picked my pocket; I pursued him to a court in Goodge-street, and found my handkerchief on him - I took him to the office, and he was committed.
GUILTY. Aged 14.

[trialAccount t18290409-265]
944. JOHN BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of March, 1 wooden till, value 1s.; 1 shilling, 1 sixpence, 17 penny-pieces, and 28 halfpence, the property of Adam Mott.
HANNAH MOTT. I am sister of Adam Mott, who lives in George-street, Euston-square. About four o'clock in the afternoon of the 16th of March, I was in the parlour, adjoining the shop, which is a grocer's; I heard what I thought was a knocking on the counter, but it proved to be the unlocking of the till; I looked, the till was gone, and I saw a person going out of the shop with it, under his arm - I believe the prisoner to be the man; I ran to the door, but he was out of sight; I saw him again in about three hours, at Marlborough-street; this is the till - there was about 8s. 3 1/2d, in it; a person could reach over the counter, and get it.
WILLIAM HAWKINS. I am an officer. On the 16th of March I was standing at my own house; the prisoner ran by with this till, and the money rattling in it; it was just after four o'clock; he was about four hundred yards from the prosecutor's - I pursued him down a mews; he threw the till into an area, at the corner of Euston-square and Drummond-street, and most of the money went into another area; I took him about one hundred yards further, without losing sight of him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was never inside the shop, and know nothing of it; I was going to a gentleman of the name of Clarkson, second mate of the Repulse; a man came behind me, and threw this till into the area - there was a cry of Stop thief! I ran after the man, and this gentleman took me.
GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-266]
945. ROBERT CARTER was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 1 goat, price 10s., the property of Randall Edward Brewin.
DANIEL GILES. I am a bird-fancier. This goat was on Mr. Chapman's premises, at the back of Barnsbury-park. Islington, on the 11th of April; there is a bank round the field; the prisoner ran, took a strap from his pocket, and led the goat away - he took it into Maiden-lane, and the officer took him; I had not known him before.
WILLIAM BERNARD. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner leading the goat along; I asked him where he was going; he said, home; I asked him where that was; he said somewhere about Holborn; I took the goat to the officer, and gave information.
WILLIAM DOOBER. I am servant to Mr. Randall Edward Brewin. The officer showed me a goat, which I knew was my master's - I know nothing of the prisoner.
Prisoner's Defence. I went after a situation at a varnish-maker's, in Liverpool-road; I was to go on Saturday for an answer - I thought I would go and cut two turfs; a man in the field asked who the goat belonged to; some men said they did not know; he then asked me if I would earn sixpence or a shilling, and take the goat with him - he called the goat, and it came; he took hold of it, put a string round it, and told me to lead it - he walked on, and went away.
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-267]
946. THOMAS CHAPMAN was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of April, 1 pair of breeches, value 4s., and 1 scarf, value 2s., the goods of James Chapman Butcher.
WILLIAM HARRISON. I am assistant to James Chapman Butcher, pawnbroker, of New Brentford. We lost this scarf and pair of breeches, on the 7th of April, from inside the shop door; I had seen the prisoner in the town.
JOHN COPE FOLKARD. I am a pawnbroker, and live at Old Brentford. About half-past eight o'clock on this evening this pair of breeches was brought to me by the prisoner; he wanted five shillings on them - I had received information; I said,
"Very well." and told my young man to make out the duplicate; I went round to try to shut the door, but he saw my intention, and ran off; I followed; he fell down, got up, and ran again.
JOSEPH DICKENSON. I was coming down the town, and heard the cry of Stop thief! I saw the prisoner running; I pursued, and caught him; as I was taking him to the cage, I saw him stoop; I looked, and found this scarf.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was at Brentford, and met a man, who asked me where there was a pawnbroker's; I went to show him; he asked me to take these things in - he said he would give me sixpence, and the ticket - he told me to ask five shillings, and if they would lend it, I was to go and tell him; I went to tell him, and the gentleman came to take me.
GUILTY. Aged. 20.
Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18290409-268]
947. THOMAS GOULBOURN and PHILIP NEWMAN were indicted for stealing, on the 8th of April, 7 lbs. of beef, value 3s. 6d., the goods of Thomas Eden Frenholm.
THOMAS EDEN FRENHOLM. I am a butcher, and live at Stoke Newington. This beef was in my shop on the 8th of April, about ten minutes before six o'clock in the evening- I saw the two prisoners walking backwards and forwards two or three times, and heard one of them say it would not do; I had seen Goulbourn before, lighting the lamps in the road; Goulbourn then came and took the heef - Newman was close by him; I was at the back of the shop; I went and took Goulbourn - Newman made off, but was taken the same evening.
JAMES CLARK. I am an officer. I was sent for, and took Goulbourn.
JOSHUA FREDERICK CLEMENTS. I am an officer. I took Newman in about an hour and a half; he made great resistance.
FRANCIS MOORE. I was in the shop. I saw the two prisoners come up together; Newman drew the beef to the edge of the window - they then went into a public-house; I then saw one of them come and take the beef - I cannot say which.
Prisoner NEWMAN. Q. Where did you see me? A. Coming along the pavement, and you drew the meat to the edge of the board.
Prisoner NEWMAN. This prisoner never saw me at all.
GOULBOURN'S Defence. This man was not with me; I never touched the meat.
GOULBOURN - GUILTY. Aged. 33.
Confined Three Months.
NEWMAN - GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-269]
948. JULIA McCUEN was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of April, 2 copper pots, value 12s., the goods of Richard Herbert.
RICHARD HERBERT. I am a boot-closer, and live in Adam and Eve-court. I lost two pots from my back kitchen - the prisoner lodged with me; I had information, gave her in charge, and on the Monday morning I found this pot at Jackson's, in Gee's-court.
THOMAS JACKSON. I live at No. 9, Gee's court, and deal in marine-stores. I bought this pot of Mr. Dobson, in Berwick-court.
JONATHAN DOBSON. I am a broker. On Saturday, between two and three o'clock, the prisoner brought this pot, and asked me if I would buy it - I told her to untie it, and asked what she wanted for it; she said 7s. - I said it was too much, which it was - it weighed about 14 lbs., with the iron; I gave her 5s. 6d. for it, and sold it for 6s.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I met a woman, who asked if I would oblige her by selling this pot, and she would allow me 1s.; she said her landlord was seizing, and her children were starving - she told me to ask 7s.; this gentleman said he would give me 5s. 6d. - I came out and told her; she said,
"Take it."
GUILTY. Aged 34.
Confined Six Weeks.

949. THOMAS MANSFIELD was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of April, 2 bit-halters, value 18s., and 1 crupper, value 2s., the goods of Charles Chamberlain.
CHARLES CHAMBERLAIN. I live at Alridge; I am a carman, and drive hay and straw to town; I lost these two bit-halters and a crupper from my stable in my yard- I left them there on the night of the 9th of April, and missed them the next morning; the door had not been locked.
SAMUEL JOHNSON. I am a saddler, and live in Turnmill street. The prisoner came and offered two bit-halters for sale, for 4s.; I gave him no answer, and he went away - I informed the constable, who took him.
JOHN ROBINSON. I am the constable. Johnson told me, and I took the prisoner - he said he had brought up two horses to the Ram inn, in Smithfield, and was going to take them home to Hertfordshire; I said if he would go there I should be satisfied, but he would not; I was forced to call in another officer - we found this crupper on him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I found the harness near Chigwell.
GUILTY. Aged. 30.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18290409-271]
950. GEORGE PALMER was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of April, I slab-stone, value 4s., the goods of Thomas Bourne.
THOMAS BOURNE. I am a baker. I missed a slabstone on Friday last from the front of a cottage in Birdcage-field.
JOHN CLAYTON. I am a silk-weaver. I was passing Bethnal-green-road, on the morning of the 9th of April, about half-past eight, or near nine o'clock; I saw the prisoner and another cross from Hart's-lane, with this slab - they set it against a wall; I went down the road, and met the officer - the prisoner was about half a mile from Mr. Bourne's.
JOHN McWILLIAMS. I am a Bow-street patrol. I received information, and went to the place - the prisonerwas standing there; Clayton said, "That is one;" he ran off - I pursued, and took him; I then went and found the slab.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I did not intend to run away. I was going to my brother - I would have taken the officer to the house.
One witness gave the prisoner a good character, and stated that he would take him into his employ.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.

953. JOSEPH ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of April, 4lbs weight of ham, value 2s., the goods of Henry Parratt.
JUDITH KING. I saw the prisoner take the ham out of Mr. Parratt's window, in London-street, and run down the street; I gave an alarm, and he was taken.
JAMES GRAY. I saw the prisoner run, and three more in front of him; I passed the prisoner, thinking the others had the ham, but they had not; I then turned, and took the prisoner with this ham, which is my master's, Mr. Henry Parratt.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This gentleman passed me, and said, "Where is that bacon?" I had not any - I took this up from the ground, and said, "I suppose this is it."

954. GEORGE MORGAN was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of April, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of John Charles Bricknell, from his person.
JOHN CHARLES BRICKNELL. I am a hosier. I was standing in a passage leading from Princes-street to Leicester-square; I felt a pressure at my pocket - I felt, and missed my handkerchief; I saw two persons near me, and took hold of one of them; a gentleman came up, and said he would point out the prisoner; he showed him to me in Coventry-street; I recognised him as one who had been behind me - on the road to the watch-house he spoke to a person, and my handkerchief was sent to the watch-house.
GEORGE WILLIAM IRESON. I saw Mr. Bricknell standing in the street; the prisoner took a handkerchief from his pocket, and put it behind him - a boy, who was standing against a post, took it from him; I am certain he is the person - I pointed him out to the prosecutor.
THOMAS GOOK. I am an officer. The prisoner was brought to me; the handkerchief was delivered to me by Harris.
JOHN HARRIS. About half-past eight o'clock I was going to get some wood; I saw a mob, and went up; the prisoner called me, and told me to go to Peter-street, to Jem Connolly, and fetch the handkerchief, which I did.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. This gentleman took another boy who had picked his pocket. I went on to St. Martin's church, and back again - he then came, and charged me with it after he had let the other boy go: I said I would go and show him where the other boy was, as I saw a boy running with it; I met Harris, and told him to go to the boy - it is quite false for that gentleman to say he saw me pick the pocket; if he did, why did he not take me.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Transported for Life.

957. ANN KING was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of March, 13 yards of printed cotton, value 7s., the goods of William Kemp Hodges.
CHARLOTTE HODGES. I am the wife of William Kemp Hodges, linen-daper, of the Minories. The prisoner came to our shop, on the 6th of March, and asked to look at some prints - I shewed her some; she looked at about three, then took a piece of cotton from the counter, and ran away with it; I gave an alarm, she was pursued, and brought back with it.
RICHARD BOOTH. I heard a cry of Stop thief! and took the prisoner, who was standing still, about one hundred yards from the shop - she had this print hanging on her arm.
WILLIAM KEMP HODGES I saw the prisoner in my shop on the 6th of March; I was in the counting-house; Mrs. Hodges made an alarm, and the prisoner was brought back - the print has my mark on it.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.

958. SUSAN JAMES was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of March, 1 handkerchief, value 1s. 6d., the goods of George Knapp.

The prisoner came in on the 30th of March to redeem an apron; she asked the price of another apron, and pulled out some others; Hare watched her -I heard something snatched from the door, and she was brought back with this handkerchief, which is mine.
GEORGE HARE. On the 30th of March I saw the prisoner pull the handkerchief from the door; she was then on the threshold - she put it under her shawl and was going off; I took hold of her - she said "Don't, don't;" I brought her back - the handkerchief has my master's mark on it. GUILTY. Aged 15.
